
# Income tax


## Chapter 03


### (IT) Illustration 1 (page 3.2)

Mr. Hari has a monthly salary of Rs. 1 lakh. Compute the amount of monthly tax to be withheld by employer?

**Answer**

estimated income → Rs 12 lakh

| Estimated tax  |     |          |
| -------------- | --- | -------- |
| First 5 lakh   | 1%  | 50,000   |
| Next 2 lakh    | 10% | 20,000   |
| Next 3 lakh    | 20% | 60,000   |
| balance 2 lakh | 30% | 60,000   |
|                |     | 1,45,000 |
TDS per month = $\frac{\text{total tax amount - tax paid}}{\text{12/ remaining month}}=\frac{1,45,000 - 0}{12} = 12,083$


<u>Case 2</u> : if salary is increased to 1.5 lakh from mangsir

estimated income = 4 x 1 lakh + 8 x 1.5 lakh = Rs 16 lakh

| Estimated tax  |     |          |
| -------------- | --- | -------- |
| First 5 lakh   | 1%  | 50,000   |
| Next 2 lakh    | 10% | 20,000   |
| Next 3 lakh    | 20% | 60,000   |
| balance 6 lakh | 30% | 1,80,000 |
|                |     | 3,10,000 |
TDS per month = $\frac{\text{total tax amount - tax paid}}{\text{12/ remaining month}}=\frac{3,10,000 - (12,083 \times 4)}{8} = 32,709$


### (IT) Illustration 3,4,5,6 (page: 3.3)

<u>Illustration-3</u>
AB & associates provided consultancy services to XYZ Ltd.  and issued an invoice of Rs 1,00,000 13% VAT . Calculate the net amount that XYZ Ltd. is liable to pay after deducting withholding tax

**Answer**

| Particulars                        | Amount   |
| ---------------------------------- | -------- |
| Audit fee                          | 1,00,000 |
| VAT amount                         | 13,000   |
| Total                              | 113,000  |
| Less: TDS @ 15% \[1,00,000 x 1.5%] | (1500)   |
| Net payment                        | 111,500  |


<u>Illustration-4</u>
Will your answer be different if XYZ ltd isn't registered in VAT

**Answer**
No, it means TDS @ 1.5% is applicable \[service provider vat मा registered छ की छैन हेर्ने हाे , service receiver जाे भएनि मत्लब भएन ]

<u>Illustration-5</u>
Will your answer be different if AB & Associates isn't registered in VAT and Total fee was Rs 1 lakh only?

**Answer**
Yes, in that case

| Particulars      | Amount   |
| ---------------- | -------- |
| Audit fee        | 1,00,000 |
| TDS @15% advance | (15000)  |
| Net payment      | 85,000   |

<u>Illustration-6</u>
Will your answer be different if the service was provided to Mr. X

**Answer**
Yes, in such case payment is made by natural person (other than proprietor) so , **NO TDS**

### (IT) Illustration 9,10 (page:3.6)

<u>Illustration -9</u>
Ms. Barsha has deposited Rs. 200 lakh on saving account of everest bank limited. She wants to know how much net interest she will receive at the end of first quarter if rate of interest was 12% pa.

**Answer**

|                                    | amount (in lakh) |
| ---------------------------------- | ---------------- |
| Total interest ( 200 x 12% x 3/12) | 6                |
| less: TDS @ 6% (final)             | (0.36)           |
| **net payment**                    | **5.64**         |

<u>Illustration - 10</u>
Will your answer be different if she had deposited the amount for business purpose i.e. in the name of her proprietorship

**Answer**

|                                    | amount (in lakh) |
| ---------------------------------- | ---------------- |
| Total interest ( 200 x 12% x 3/12) | 6                |
| less: TDS @ 15% (Advance)          | (0.9)            |
| **net payment**                    | **5.1**          |

<u>Illustration - 11</u>
Mr sagar purchased 5,000 kitta of 10% debentures of a listed company amounting to Rs 5,00,000. What shall be net interest to be received by him at the year end?

**Answer**

|                                  | amount (Rs) |
| -------------------------------- | ----------- |
| Total interest ( 500,000 x 10% ) | 50,000      |

**Answer**

|                                  | amount (Rs) |
| -------------------------------- | ----------- |

| less: TDS @ 6% (final)           | (3,000)     |
| **net payment**                  | **47,000**  |

### (IT) Illustration 13 (page:3.7)

Samanantar Private Limited is providing interest payment and loan processing fee of Rs. 2 crore and Rs. 80 lakh respectively to Everest Bank Limited. Compute the withholding tax required to be deducted by the co.

**Answer**

|                                           | amount (Rs)     |
| ----------------------------------------- | --------------- |
| Total interest                            | 2,00,00,000     |
| Service fee                               | 80,00,000       |
| less: TDS on interest                     | (0)             |
| less: TDS on service fee @ 1.5% (advance) | (1,20,000)      |
| **net payment**                           | **2,78,80,000** |
### (IT) Example questions 1 (TDS on interest)


TDS Rates for Interest Payments by Nabil Bank

| S.N. | Interest Paid to                      | TDS Rates     | Remarks / Handwritten Notes                                                                             |
| ---- | ------------------------------------- | ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| a)   | Mr. Mohan                             | 6% (final)    |                                                                                                         |
| b)   | Kamana & associates                   | 15% (advance) |                                                                                                         |
| c)   | Raman p. ltd.                         | 15% (advance) |                                                                                                         |
| d)   | Siddhartha Bank ltd.                  | No TDS        | कसैले पनि bank लाई int. pay गर्दा TDS लाग्दैन (No TDS is applicable when paying interest to any bank).  |
| e)   | Hamro sahakari sanstha ltd.           | 15% (adv.)    | co-operatives ले each other लाई दिए मात्र नलाग्ने हो (Exempt only if cooperatives pay to each other).   |
| f)   | Nepal life insurance co.              | 5% (adv.)     |                                                                                                         |
| g)   | Shikhar general insurance co.         | 15% (adv.)    | life insurance मा मात्रै 5% (5% only applies to Life Insurance).                                        |
| h)   | Pasupati Area development Trust.      | 15% final     | Typically, payments to such trusts are tax-exempt but earning interest is not part of trust's objective |
| i)   | HDFC Bank, India                      |               |                                                                                                         |
|      | → if investment in prescribed sector. | 10% (final)   | Non resident लाई pay गरेको भएर final                                                                    |
|      | → otherwise                           | 15% (final)   | Non resident लाई pay गरेको भएर final                                                                    |
specific rules नभएको कारण , interest को general rule अनुसार 15% advance लाग्छ 

Key Takeaways from the Notes:
 * Bank-to-Bank: Payments made to banks are generally not subject to TDS.
 * Insurance: There is a distinction between Life Insurance (5%) and General Insurance (15%) regarding interest TDS.
 * Individuals: For individual residents, interest from deposits in banks is usually a final withholding tax.
Would you like me to help you calculate the specific tax amount for any of these cases based on a sample principal amount?



### (IT) Example 1 (page:3.8)

<center> Balance sheet </center>

| Liabilities | Book value | Market value | Assets | Book value | market value |
| ----------- | ---------- | ------------ | ------ | ---------- | ------------ |
| capital     | 50L        |              | Assets | 100L       | 120L         |
| Reserves    | 30L        |              |        |            |              |
| liabilities | 20L        | 22L          |        |            |              |
|             | **100L**       |              |        | **100L**       |              |
Maximum dividend = 12L - 22L -50L = Rs 48L
- Realized gain = Reserve and surplus = Rs 30L
- unrealized gain = 48L - 30L = Rs 18L

What if the company sold a car worth Rs 80L for Rs 20L. to its shareholder
=> since, the car is sold at discount of 60L to shareholder . It means 60L is the payment in the capacity of beneficiary . i.e. 60L is dividend as per Income tax act.

=> But max dividend limit is only Rs 48L . so ,
-  Rs 48L = dividend
- Rs 12L = Repayment of capital


### (IT) Illustration 14 (page:3.9)

ABC Private Limited is declaring cash dividend of Rs. 100 lakhs. compute withholding tax 

Answer


| particulars       | amount (Rs in lakh) |
| ----------------- | ------------------- |
| dividend          | 100                 |
| TDS (@5% final)   | (5)                 |
| **total payment** | **95**              |

Hence, the tax to be withheld will be Rs. 5 lakh
### (IT) Illustration 15 (page:3.9)

ABC P.ltd declared to issue 50,000 units of bonus shares of Rs.100 each. Is there any implications of withholding tax ?

Answer


> [!NOTE] 
> Company must pay bonus share of 50,000. Bonus share cannot be given proportionately (i.e. Bonus share काटेर दिन मिल्दैन ) so.
> 1. option 1: recipient must pay tax and receive the bonus share
> 2. option 2: company will pay the tax and record it as cash dividend

journal entry 
<u>option 1</u>
Bank alc .......Dr               2,63,158
	To TDS payable                    2,63,158

Reserve and surplus alc ..Dr         50,00,000
	To Bonus shares alc                             50,00,000


<u>option 2</u>
Reserve and surplus alc ..Dr         52,63,158
	To Bonus shares alc                             50,00,000
	TDS payable alc                                    2,63,158
>company shall declare to distribute
>1. 50,000,Bonus share of Rs 100 and
>2. cash dividend of 2,63,158 (for tax purpose)

| particulars                                                       | amount (Rs in lakh) |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- |
| Bonus share (50,000 units x Rs 100)                               | 50                  |
| cash dividend $\left( \frac{50,00,000}{0.95} \times 0.05 \right)$ | <u>2.63158</u>      |
| **Total dividend**                                                | **52.63158**        |
| TDS (@5% final)                                                   | (2.63158)           |
| **total payment**                                                     | **50**                  |
hence, the tax must be withhold @5% final which amounts to Rs 2,63,158

Apex Private Limited decided to distribute 1 I-phone worth Rs. 200,000 to each of its 3 shareholders. Is there any implication of withholding tax

hence, the tax must be withhold @5% final which amounts to Rs 31,579



### (IT) Illustration 17 (page:3.10)

A & Co Ltd decided to declare and distribute the cash dividend at the rate of 10% amounting to Rs 50,000 and bonus shares at the rate of 20% amounting to Rs 1,00,000. Compute withholding tax.

Answer

| particulars                         | amount (Rs)   |
| ----------------------------------- | ------------- |
| Bonus share (50,000 units x Rs 100) | 1,00,000      |
| cash dividend                       | <u>50,000</u> |
| **Total dividend**                  | **1,50,000**  |
| TDS (@5% final)                     | (7,500)       |
| **total payment**                       | **14,2,500**      |
hence, the tax must be withhold @5% final which amounts to Rs 7,500. Out of which bonus share of 1,00,000 and cash dividend of 42,500 is received by the shareholder


### (IT) Illustration 18,19,20 (page:3.10)

Siddartha P. Ltd. holds 60% shares in Lumbini P. Ltd. During the year, Lumbini P. Ltd. declared to distribute cash dividend of Rs. 100 lakhs. Siddartha P. Ltd. doesn't have any other income and decided to declare entire income as dividend. Compute withholding tax. 

Answer

**Dividend distributed by Lumbini P. Ltd.**

| particulars       | amount (Rs in lakh) |
| ----------------- | ------------------- |
| total dividend    | 100                 |
| TDS (@5% final)   | (5)                 |
| **total payment** | **95**              |


**Dividend distributed by Siddartha P. Ltd.**

| Particulars                              | amount(Rs in lakhs) |
| ---------------------------------------- | ------------------- |
| dividend from Lumbini P.Ltd. (60% of 95) | 57                  |
| income of siddartha P.Ltd                | <u>   0   </u>      |
| **Total income**                         | **57**              |
| TDS (no TDS to avoid double tax)         | (0)                 |
| **Total payment**                        | **57**              |


<u>(IT) Illustration 19 (page:3.10)</u>
What if P. Ltd also has business income of Rs. 40 Lakhs in illustration 18?

Answer
**Dividend distributed by Siddartha P. Ltd.**

| Particulars                  | amount(Rs in lakhs) |
| ---------------------------- | ------------------- |
| dividend from Lumbini P.Ltd. | 57                  |
| income of siddartha P.Ltd    | <u>   40   </u>     |
| **Total income**             | **97**              |
| TDS (5% of 40lakh)           | (2)                 |
| **Total payment**            | **95**              |


<u>(IT) Illustration 20 (page:3.10)</u>
What if Siddartha P.Ltd. decided to declare dividend of Rs. 40 lakhs only in illustration 19

Answer
**Dividend distributed by Siddartha P. Ltd.**

| Particulars                  | amount(Rs in lakhs) |
| ---------------------------- | ------------------- |
| dividend from Lumbini P.Ltd. | 57                  |
| income of siddartha P.Ltd    | <u>   40   </u>     |
| **Total income**             | **97**              |
|                              |                     |
| dividend distributed         | 40                  |
| TDS (WN1)                    | (82,474)            |
| **Total payment**            | **39.17525**        |
**WN1**
percentage own income distributed as dividend = $\frac{\text{distributed amount}}{\text{total amount}}$ =  $\frac{40,00,000}{97,00,000}$ = 41.24%
TDS = 5% of 41.24% of total dividend = 5% x 41.24% x 40,00,000 = Rs 82,474
> out of 40 lakh 
> 41.24% → shall deduct TDS @5%
> 58.76% → no TDS to avoid double taxation


### (IT) Illustration 21 (page:3.11)

Ms. Samikshya wants to do MBA from Cambridge university. Registration fee is $10,000. Prevailing exchange rate is $1 = Rs. 130. She wants to know whether there's any obligation of withholding tax.

Answer

Since, Samikshya must pay $10,000 on net to cambridge for registration, samikshya should also withheld and pay the tax in addition to $10,000 dollar 

i.e.
tax amount = $\frac{\$ 10,000}{0.95} \times 5\% \times Rs.130$ = 68,421

### (IT) Illustration 22 (page:3.12)

Mr. Prachanda is earning Rs. 30,000/meeting. what shall be withholding tax implication in this case

Answer

TDS =  15%(advance) of 30,000/meeting = Rs. 4,500 (advance)

#### (IT) Question 31 chapter 3 (page: 3.19)

 31. X & Co. made the following payment to Z & Co. under a contract for the supply of materials required for its company. Both the company are registered under VAT.
 
| Date | Amount (Rs) |
|---|---|
| 2069.04.08 | Rs 25,000 |
| 2069.04.12 | Rs 10,000 |
| 2069.04.13 | Rs 5,000 |
| 2069.04.15 | Rs 10,000 |
| 2069.04.17 | Rs 5,000 |
Since the payments are less than Rs 50,000, X & Co. claims no tax shall be deducted.


**Answer** 

According to Section 89(1) of Income Tax Act, 2058, tax shall be withheld @ 1.5% of the total payment if the payment > 50,000 under a contract.
Further, Sec. 89(2) states that the payment of 50,000 is computed by aggregating the payment with the payment made by contractee or its associated person to contractor or its associated person in past 10 days.
In the given case, X & Co. has made various payments under a contract for supply of materials. Thus, it’s a payment covered u/s 89.
Applicability of tax withholding & its computation is as below:

| Particulars                    | 04-08  | 04-12  | 04-13  | 04-15                              | 04-17  |
| ------------------------------ | ------ | ------ | ------ | ---------------------------------- | ------ |
| a) Payment being made today    | 25,000 | 10,000 | 5,000  | 10,000                             | 5,000  |
| b) Payment during last 10 days | —      | 25,000 | 35,000 | 40,000                             | 50,000 |
| Total                          | 25,000 | 35,000 | 40,000 | 50,000                             | 55,000 |
|                                |        |        |        | (50k exceed भयो भने मात्रै लाग्ने) |        |
| TDS rate                       | N.A.   | N.A.   | N.A.   | N.A.                               | 1.5%   |
| TDS Amount                     | —      | —      | —      | —                                  | 825    |

> Note: The TDS of 825 on 04-17 is calculated as 1.5\% of the total accumulated payment (55,000 \times 0.015 = 825).

### (IT) Illustration 1 (page: 4.1)

Illustration 1: Private Building Determination
Goal: Determine whether the asset qualifies as a Private Building based on land area and usage.

| Total Assets                | Sold Assets          | Private Building      |
| --------------------------- | -------------------- | --------------------- |
| Building + 7 aana + 5 aana* | Building + 12 aana#  | ✓                     |
| Building + 5 aana + 7 aana  | Building + 12 aana   | Private up to 10 aana |
| Building + 10 aana + 8 aana | Building + 18 aana   | Private up to 16 aana |
| Building + 18 aana          | Building + 18 aana   | Private up to 16 aana |
| Building + 7 aana + 5 aana  | Building + 7 aana    | ✓ Private             |
| Building (Apartment)        | Building (Apartment) | ✓                     |
| Land - 12 aana              | Land - 12 aana       | X                     |
\* It means the land occupied by the building is 7 aana and extra space is 5 aana. 
\# Including the land occupied by the building.


### (IT) Illustration 2,3 (page: 4.3)

Mr. Ram made an agreement to sale a land for Rs. 52 lakhs and received Rs. 30 lakhs as baina. He had purchased the land for Rs. 20 lakhs. After 2 months, he received the balance amount and transfer the ownership to the buyer. Is there any tax implications?

**Answer**

<u>before 2 month</u>

|       | Particulars           | Amount          |
| ----- | --------------------- | --------------- |
|       | incoming              | 30L             |
| Less: | outgoing              | <u>20L</u>      |
|       | net gain on disposal  | 10L             |
|       | advance tax (5%/7.5%) | 50,000 / 75,000 |

<u>after 2 month</u>

|       | Particulars           | Amount              |
| ----- | --------------------- | ------------------- |
|       | incoming              | 22L                 |
| Less: | outgoing              | <u>0</u>            |
|       | net gain on disposal  | 22L                 |
|       | advance tax (5%/7.5%) | 1,10,000 / 1,65,000 |
or

|       | Particulars           | Amount              |
| ----- | --------------------- | ------------------- |
|       | incoming              | 52L                 |
| Less: | outgoing (20L+10L)    | <u>30L</u>          |
|       | net gain on disposal  | 22L                 |
|       | advance tax (5%/7.5%) | 1,10,000 / 1,65,000 |




What if he had received only Rs. 12 lakh as advance and balance amount on the transfer of ownership in example 1?

**Answer**

<u>before 2 month</u>

|       | Particulars           | Amount     |
| ----- | --------------------- | ---------- |
|       | incoming              | 12L        |
| Less: | outgoing              | <u>20L</u> |
|       | net gain on disposal  | 0          |
|       | advance tax (5%/7.5%) | 0          |

<u>after 2 month</u>

|       | Particulars           | Amount              |
| ----- | --------------------- | ------------------- |
|       | incoming              | 40L                 |
| Less: | outgoing(20L-12L)     | <u>8</u>            |
|       | net gain on disposal  | 32L                 |
|       | advance tax (5%/7.5%) | 1,60,000 / 2,40,000 |
or

|       | Particulars           | Amount              |
| ----- | --------------------- | ------------------- |
|       | incoming              | 52L                 |
| Less: | outgoing              | <u>20L</u>          |
|       | net gain on disposal  | 32L                 |
|       | advance tax (5%/7.5%) | 1,60,000 / 2,40,000 |


### (IT) Illustration 4 (page: 4.3)

 A debtor of Rs. 20 lakh became bad debt. What shall be tax implications?
**Answer**
The tax payer shall apply all the necessary/reasonable steps before writing it off as bad debt. 
Even then if it is a bad debt then tax implications shall be as follows:

|       | Particulars          | Amount     |
| ----- | -------------------- | ---------- |
|       | incoming             | 0          |
| Less: | outgoing             | <u>20L</u> |
|       | net gain on disposal | (20L)      |
above loss of 20L shall be deducted in other profits (for tax saving) as per [[Income tax act 2002(2058)#IT act Loss setoff carried forward and set off|section 20]]
### (IT) Illustration 5 (page: 4.4)

ABC private limited was holding a land costing Rs. 90 lakhs for investment purpose. Later, it started a real estate business and held the land for sale. Market value as on such date was Rs. 110 lakhs. Finally, it was sold for Rs. 122 lakhs. Show the tax implications,

**Answer**
before starting real estate business, it was Business asset 
After starting real estate business, that land is now converted into trading asset 
As per section 40(3d) of income tax act 2058, if any person started using depreciable assets, trading assets, business assets in a manner to change it's type , then it is considered as deemed disposal 
hence, 

|       | Particulars          | Amount     |
| ----- | -------------------- | ---------- |
|       | incoming             | 110        |
| Less: | outgoing             | <u>90L</u> |
|       | net gain on disposal | 20L        |

on the date of sales

|       | Particulars          | Amount      |
| ----- | -------------------- | ----------- |
|       | incoming             | 122L        |
| Less: | outgoing             | <u>110L</u> |
|       | net gain on disposal | 12L         |

### (IT) Illustration 6 (page: 4.4)

Illustration 6: Shareholding Structure
Company: Hanuman Private Limited
Question: Is there any tax implication based on the change in shareholding?

| Shareholders | 2076.05.17 | 2079.05.16 |
|---|---|---|
| A | 30% | 0% |
| B | 30% | 0% |
| C | 40% | 40% |
| D | - | 60% |

**Answer**
As per section 40(3e) of income tax act 2058, if the total change in the ownership of an entity from last 3 years is ==50% or more== then the entity is deemed to be disposed at market value
Tax implication

| Shareholders | 2076.05.17 | 2079.05.16 | decrease | increase |
| ------------ | ---------- | ---------- | -------- | -------- |
| A            | 30%        | 0%         | (30%)    |          |
| B            | 30%        | 0%         | (30%)    |          |
| C            | 40%        | 40%        | (0%)     |          |
| D            |            |            |          | 60%      |
| total change |            |            | 60%      | 60%      |
since the change is 60% which is greater than 50% . So the entity will be deemed disposed

### (IT) Illustration 10 (page: 4.8)

Mr. Ramesh is involved in following shares transactions of a listed company. Calculate net amount receivable by him on disposal of shares if brokerage @ 0.1% is charged on both purchase and sale of shares.
 * 2078.04.15: Purchase of shares: 500 kitta @ 250/kitta
 * 2078.06.18: Purchase of shares: 700 kitta @ 280/kitta
 * 2079.08.25: Sold: 800 kitta @ 300/kitta
Alternative Scenario: What if 700 kitta were purchased on 2079.01.10 instead of 2078.06.18?

**Answer**

outgoing rate = $\frac{(\text{500 kitta} \times \text{Rs 250}) + (\text{700 kitta} \times \text{Rs 280})}{500 + 700} = \text{Rs 267.5 per share}$


|       | Particulars                             | Amounts    |
| ----- | --------------------------------------- | ---------- |
|       | incomming ( 800 kitta x Rs 300)         | 240,000    |
| less: | outgoing                                |            |
|       | purchase cost (800 kitta x Rs 267.5)    | 2,14,000   |
|       | Brokerage on purchase (0.1% of 214,000) | 214        |
|       | Brokerage on sales (0.1% of 240,000)    | <u>240</u> |
|       | net gain on disposal of shares u/s 39   | 25,546     |
|       | Advance tax ==@5%==                     |            |

==In alternative scenario , Advance tax for 500 shares is 5% and for 700 shares is 7.5% so we have to use FIFO method==

|       | Particulars                              | Amounts    |
| ----- | ---------------------------------------- | ---------- |
|       | incomming ( 500 kitta x Rs 300)          | 1,50,000   |
| less: | outgoing                                 |            |
|       | purchase cost (500 kitta x Rs 250)       | 1,25,000   |
|       | Brokerage on purchase (0.1% of 1,25,000) | 125        |
|       | Brokerage on sales (0.1% of 1,50,000)    | <u>150</u> |
|       | net gain on disposal of shares u/s 39    | 24,725     |
|       | Advance tax ==@5%==                      | 1236.25    |
|       |                                          |            |
|       | incomming ( 300 kitta x Rs 300)          | 90,000     |
| less: | outgoing                                 |            |
|       | purchase cost (300 kitta x Rs 280)       | 84,000     |
|       | Brokerage on purchase (0.1% of 84,000)   | 84         |
|       | Brokerage on sales (0.1% of 90,000)      | <u>90</u>  |
|       | net gain on disposal of shares u/s 39    | 5,826      |
|       | Advance tax ==@7.5%==                    | 436.95     |



### (IT) Illustration 11 (page: 4.8)

ABC pvt ltd has following shares transactions of a listed company. Calculate advance tax collection on disposal of shares
 * 2077.06.17: Purchased 2000 kitta @ 820/share
 * 2078.02.18: Bonus share 1 for 2 held
 * 2079.07.04: Sold 2,500 kitta @ 800/share

**Answer**
bonus share =  1/2 of 2000 = 1,000 kitta

outgoing rate = $\frac{(\text{2000 kitta} \times \text{Rs 820}) + (\text{1000 kitta} \times \text{Rs 100})}{2000 + 1000} = \text{Rs 580 per share}$


|       | Particulars                            | Amounts  |
| ----- | -------------------------------------- | -------- |
|       | incomming ( 2500 kitta x Rs 800)       | 20L      |
| less: | outgoing                               |          |
|       | purchase cost (2500 kitta x Rs 546.67) | 14,50,00 |
|       | net gain on disposal of shares u/s 39  | 5,50,000 |
|       | Advance tax ==@10%==                   | 55,000   |

>[!tip] cost of bonus share 
>Issued before 2058.12.19 → market value on 2058.12.19
>Issued after 2058.12.19 → Rs 100 per share

#### Question 11 chapter 4 (page: 4.12)

11. Mr Ramesh, a resident natural person has loss from Sale of Share amounting Rs. 200,000 for the financial year 2065-66. He also incurred loss of Rs. 300,000 from another furniture business. Same loss has not been set off with any other income till date. In F.Y. 2064-65 also he has incurred loss of Rs. 200,000 from said furniture business. He sold the land used for personal purpose for Rs. 6,000,000. Same land was purchased by him four years back for Rs. 2,000,000. Show tax treatment.

**Answer**
Calculation of net gain from sale of asset as per sec 39

|       | Particulars     | Amounts     |
| ----- | --------------- | ----------- |
|       | incoming        | 60,00,000   |
| Less: | outgoing        | (20,00,000) |
|       | net gain u/s 39 | 40,00,000   |

Calculation of loss set off from NBCA u/s 36(2)

|       | Particulars                               | Amounts           |
| ----- | ----------------------------------------- | ----------------- |
|       | net gain from sale of NBCA u/s 39         | 40,00,000         |
| Less: | Unrelieved loss from investment           | (2,00,000)        |
| Less: | Unrelieved loss from disposal of Business | (3,00,000)        |
| Less: | P.Y Unrelieved loss from  investment      | 0                 |
| Less: | P.Y Unrelieved loss from Business assets  | <u>(2,00,000)</u> |
| =     | net gain u/s 36(2)                        | 33,00,000         |
Hence, the gain from sale of NBCA u/s 36(2) shall be transferred to income from investment u/s 9

| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Business Income | Rs. 19 lakhs | Rs. 2 lakh |
| Gain on disposal of Land - Classified as NBCA (Disposal Value: Rs. 22 lakhs) | Rs. 10 lakhs | Rs. 10 lakh |

 * What if the land was classified as business assets?


**Answer**

Computation of tax liability of Mr. Ramnarayan for the IY 2079-80

|     | Particulars                                                  | Case A       | Case B           |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------ | ---------------- |
| A   | income from NBCA disposal                                    | 10L          | 10L              |
| B   | Advance tax (5%) <br>Assumption : asset owned for $\geq$ 5yr | 50,000       | 50,000           |
|     |                                                              |              |                  |
| C   | Income from business                                         | 19L          | 2L               |
| D   | Basic exemption limit unused                                 | 0            | 3L(5L-2L)        |
| E   | Tax credit on disposal of NBCA                               |              | 15,000 (3L x 5%) |
|     |                                                              |              |                  |
|     | net business income (C-D)                                    | 19L          | 0                |
|     |                                                              |              |                  |
| F   | Tax amount                                                   |              |                  |
|     | 0-5L/2L = 0%                                                 | 0            | 0                |
|     | 5L-7L = 10%                                                  | 20,000       | -                |
|     | 7L-10L = 20%                                                 | 60,000       | -                |
|     | 10L - 19L = 30%                                              | 2,70,000     | -                |
|     | total tax amount                                             | 3,50,000     | 0                |
|     |                                                              |              |                  |
|     | net tax amount (F +B - E )                                   | 4,00,000     | 35,000           |
| -   | tax paid                                                     | (50,000)     | (50,000)         |
| =   | **net tax laibility**                                        | **3,50,000** | **(15,000)**     |


If land was classified as business asset

Computation of tax liability of Mr. Ramnarayan for the IY 2079-80

|     | Particulars                          | Case A       | Case B       |
| --- | ------------------------------------ | ------------ | ------------ |
| A   | disposal value of land               | 22L          | 22L          |
| B   | Advance tax (1.5% of disposal value) | 33,000       | 33,000       |
|     |                                      |              |              |
| C   | Income from business                 | 19L          | 2L           |
| D   | income from business asset           | 10L          | 10L          |
| E   | Total income (C+D)                   | 29L          | 12L          |
|     |                                      |              |              |
| F   | Tax amount                           |              |              |
|     | 0 - 5L = 0%                          | 0            | 0            |
|     | 5L - 7L = 10%                        | 20,000       | 20,000       |
|     | 7L - 10L = 20%                       | 60,000       | 60,000       |
|     | 10L - 20L/12L = 30%                  | 3,00,000     | 60,000       |
|     | 20L - 29L = 36%                      | 3,24,000     | -            |
|     | Total tax amount                     | 7,04,000     | 1,40,000     |
|     |                                      |              |              |
|     | Total tax amount                     | 7,04,000     | 1,40,000     |
| -   | tax paid                             | (33,000)     | (33,000)     |
| =   | **net tax laibility**                | **6,71,000** | **1,07,000** |


#### Question 15 chapter 4 (page: 4.12)

15. Compute advance tax collection amount on sale of listed shares by Ramjaan Private Limited as below:

| Date       | Transaction                  | Details         |
| ---------- | ---------------------------- | --------------- |
| 2054.12.07 | Purchased - 500 kitta shares | @ Rs 200/shares |
| 2056.07.18 | Bonus shares - 1 for 2 held  |                 |
| 2058.12.19 | Market Value                 | @ Rs 220/share  |
| 2064.06.04 | Purchased - 300 kitta        | @ Rs. 400/share |
| 2072.01.11 | Sold - 450 shares            | @ Rs 520/share  |
| 2076.04.28 | Bonus share - 1 for 2 held   |                 |
| 2078.07.19 | Purchase - 300 kitta         | @ Rs 600/share  |
| 2078.12.15 | Bonus share - 1 for 5 held   |                 |
| 2079.09.07 | Sold - 800 shares            | @ Rs 720/share  |

**Answer**


| Date       | Particulars         | Amount       |
| ---------- | ------------------- | ------------ |
| 2072.01.11 | Sales (WN1)         | 2,34,000     |
|            | cost of shares(WN1) | (1,22,143.5) |
|            | net gain            | 1,11,856.5   |
|            | advance tax (@10%)  | 11,185.6     |


| Date       | Particulars         | Amount    |
| ---------- | ------------------- | --------- |
| 2079.09.07 | Sales (WN1)         | 5,76,000  |
|            | cost of shares(WN1) | (220,480) |
|            | net gain            | 3,55,520  |
|            | advance tax (@10%)  | 35,552    |


**WN1**

| Date       | buy/sell/bonus                                  | rate     | value    | total shares          | total value                                         | weighted average rate  |
| ---------- | ----------------------------------------------- | -------- | -------- | --------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
| 2058.12.19 | -                                               | 220 (MV) |          | 750 (500 + 1/2 x 500) | 1,65,000                                            |                        |
| 2064.06.04 | 300                                             | 400      | 1,20,000 | 1050                  | 2,85,000<br>(1,65,000 + 1,20,000)                   | 271.43 (2,85,000/1050) |
| 2072.01.11 | <span style="color:rgb(192, 0, 0)">(450)</span> | 520      | -        | 600                   | 1,62,858                                            |                        |
| 2076.04.28 | 300 (1/2 $\times$ 600)                          | 100      | 30,000   | 900                   |                                                     |                        |
| 2078.07.19 | 300                                             | 600      | 1,80,000 | 1200                  |                                                     |                        |
| 2078.12.15 | 240 (1/5 $\times$ 1200)                         | 100      | 24,000   | 1440                  | 3,96,858<br>(1,62,858 + 30,000 + 1,80,000 + 24,000) | 275.60 (3,96,858/1440) |
| 2079.09.07 | <span style="color:rgb(192, 0, 0)">(800)</span> | 720      | -        | 640                   |                                                     |                        |

>[!tip]
>- make table as WN1 and 
>- calculate total value on 2058.12.19
>- calculate the weighted average rate and total value just before sales only 
>- while calculating total value before sale , don't forget to add total value after previous sale 
>  i.e (1,62,858 + 30,000 + 1,80,000 + 24,000) => don't forget 1,62,858
>- total value after sales = weighted average rate $\times$ total shares left
>- do not calculate value of sales

## Chapter 05


### (IT) Illustration 1 (page: 5.1)

Illustration 1
Mr. Baburam recently joined a new company on a monthly remuneration of Rs. 1 lakh/month, 60% of which is basic salary. His employer additionally contributes to PF as per law. How much amount will he receive per month. Also, record journal entry in the books of the company. Ignore TDS.

**Answer**


|     | Particulars                              | amount            |
| --- | ---------------------------------------- | ----------------- |
|     | basic salary                             | 7,20,000          |
| +   | other allowances                         | 4,80,000          |
| +   | PF contribution by employer (10% of 12L) | <u>72,000</u>     |
| =   | total income                             | 12,72,000         |
| -   | total PF contribution (20% of 12L)       | <u>(1,44,000)</u> |
| =   | net recievable                           | 11,28,000         |

<u>Journal entry</u>
salary alc Dr                         12,72,000
`   ` To salary payable alc                    11,28,00
`   ` To PF payable alc                         1,44,000 



### (IT) Illustration 2,3,4 (page: 5.2)
Illustration 2
Mr. Tarak recently joined a new company on a monthly remuneration of Rs. 1 lakh/month. His employer additionally contributes to SSF as per law. Calculate allowable reduction for contribution to the fund and taxable income of Mr. Tarak for the income year 2079.

**Answer**

|     | Particulars                    | Amount      |
| --- | ------------------------------ | ----------- |
|     | salary                         | 12L         |
| +   | employer's contribution to SSF | <u>2.4L</u> |
| =   | assessable income              | 14.4L       |
|     | <u>deduction</u>               |             |
| -   | contribution to SSF (WN1)      | (3,72,000)  |
| =   | taxable income                 | 10,68,000   |
**WN1** : deduction for contribution in retirement fund
lower of 
1/3 of AI = 1/3 x 14.4L = 4,80,000
or 
5lakh 
or 
actual contribution  =  31% of 12L = 3,72,000

>[!warning]
>employer's contribution is not part of basic salary or remuneration
>stop overthinking and just follow the pattern



Illustration 3
Will your answer be different if the employer and employee contribute to PF as per law?

**Answer**

|     | Particulars                   | Amount      |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ----------- |
|     | salary                        | 12L         |
| +   | employer's contribution to PF | <u>1.2L</u> |
| =   | assessable income             | 13.2L       |
|     | <u>deduction</u>              |             |
| -   | contribution to PF (WN1)      | (2,40,000)  |
| =   | taxable income                | 10,80,000   |
**WN1** : deduction for contribution in retirement fund
lower of 
1/3 of AI = 1/3 x 13.2L = 4,40,000
or 
5lakh 
or 
actual contribution  =  20% of 12L = 2,40,000



Illustration 4
Will your answer be different if employer and employee contribute to unapproved retirement fund?

**Answer**

|     | Particulars                     | Amount      |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ----------- |
|     | salary                          | 12L         |
| +   | employer's contribution to fund | <u>1.2L</u> |
| =   | assessable income               | 13.2L       |
|     | <u>deduction</u>                |             |
| -   | contribution to fund            | (0)         |
| =   | taxable income                  | 13.2L       |





### (IT) Illustration 5,6,7 (page: 5.3)
Illustration 5
Mr. Ramesh retired from a government job and has gratuity receivable of Rs. 22 lakh. If the amount accrued as on 2058-12-19 was Rs. 4 lakh. Calculate final withholding.

**Answer**

|     | Particulars                               | Amount      |
| --- | ----------------------------------------- | ----------- |
|     | total receivable                          | 22L         |
| -   | receivable before 2058.12.19              | <u>(4L)</u> |
| =   | gain                                      | 18L         |
| -   | deduction u/s 65 (WN1)                    | <u>9L</u>   |
| =   | taxable amount (final withholding income) | 9L          |
| =   | tax (5%)\[section 88(1(1))]               | 0.45L       |
**WN1**:
higher of 
50% of 18L = 9L
or 
5L




Illustration 6
Will your answer be different if he was retired from other than a government job?

**Answer**
Yes,

|     | Particulars                  | Amount      |
| --- | ---------------------------- | ----------- |
|     | total receivable             | 22L         |
| -   | receivable before 2058.12.19 | <u>(4L)</u> |
| =   | gain                         | 18L         |
| =   | tax (15%)\[section 88(1)]    | 2.7L        |


Illustration 7
Will your answer be different if it was received from an unapproved retirement fund and total contribution deposited in the fund after commencement of the Act is Rs. 14 lakh?
Would you like me to help you solve any of these illustrations?

**Answer**
No, gratuity is non contributory payment, so contribution to retirement fund is not relevant for calculation of tax on payment of gratuity. 

But if it was contributory payment from unapproved retirement fund:

|     | Particulars                     | Amount       |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ------------ |
|     | total receivable                | 22L          |
| -   | receivable before 2058.12.19    | (4L)         |
| -   | contribution to retirement fund | <u>(14L)</u> |
| =   | gain                            | 4L           |
| =   | tax (15%)\[section 88(2c)]      | 0.2L         |

#### (IT) Question 4 chapter 5 (page: 5.6)


4. Mr. Dibij was working in a private company with following monthly remunerations:
 * Basic Salary: Rs. 60,000
 * Allowances: 50% of basic salary
 * Contributions to PF as per law
He left the job on Poush 29, 2079 & joined a new co. on very next day with following salary structure:
 * Basic Salary: Rs. 80,000
 * Allowances: Rs. 20,000
 * Contributions to SSF as per law.
Calculate taxable income for the year if he additionally contributed Rs. 120,000 to CIT as per law.
What if Contribution to CIT was Rs. 420,000?

**Answer**

|     | Particulars                                        | amount            | amount            |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------- | ----------------- | ----------------- |
|     | basic salary (6x (60,000 + 80,000))                | 8,40,000          | 8,40,000          |
| +   | other allowances(6x (30,000 + 20,000))             | 3,00,000          | 3,00,000          |
| +   | PF contribution by employer (10% of (60,000 x 6))  | 36,000            | 36,000            |
| +   | SSF contribution by employer (20% of (80,000 x 6)) | <u>96,000</u>     | <u>96,000</u>     |
| =   | assessable income                                  | 12,72,000         | 12,72,000         |
|     | <u>deduction</u>                                   |                   |                   |
| -   | contribution to fund (WN1)                         | <u>(3,40,800)</u> | <u>(4,24,000)</u> |
| =   | taxable income                                     | 9,31,200          | 8,48,000          |
**WN1** : deduction for contribution in retirement fund
lower of 
1/3 of AI = 1/3 x 12,72,000 = 4,24,000
or 
5lakh 
or 
actual contribution  = 31% of (6x 80,000) + 20% of (6 x 60,000) + 1,20,000 = 3,40,800

**if his actual contribution to CIT is 4,20,000 then**

**WN1** : deduction for contribution in retirement fund
lower of 
1/3 of AI = 1/3 x 12,72,000 = 4,24,000
or 
5lakh 
or 
actual contribution  = 31% of (6x 80,000) + 20% of (6 x 60,000) + 4,20,000 = 6,40,800

## Chapter 06




### Questions


#### (IT) Question 1 chapter 6 (page: 6.5)

 1. Mr. Mahesh is engaged in a grocery business in Kathmandu Metropolitan city with annual turnover and income of Rs. 28 and Rs. 2 lakhs respectively. Calculate tax liability.
   Will your answer be different if he has suffered a loss of Rs. 1 lakh from the business?

**Answer**
As per section 4(4), he is a small tax payer having annual turnover not more than 30 lakh and profit not more than 3 lakh , so he should pay tax as per section 1(17) of schedule 1
tax liability = Rs 7,500
No, small tax payer should pay tax even if he/she suffers loss


#### (IT) Question 2 chapter 6 (page: 6.5)

 2. Mr Ramesh has been operating a wholesale shop in Lalitpur. For the FY 2078/79 his sales income in business was Rs 24 lakhs and his taxable profit was Rs 2,40,000. During the course of business he had supplied goods for Rs 60,000 to a company and had received a net payment after deduction of advance tax 1.5%. i.e. Rs 900. That year his approved medical expense was Rs 2,000. Determine the tax implication. Does he need to file estimated tax return?

**Answer**
As per section 4(4), he is a small tax payer having annual turnover not more than 30 lakh and profit not more than 3 lakh , he cannot claim TDS or medical tax credit , and small tax payer must pay tax on turnover basis. 
Therefore, 
tax liability = Rs 7,500

As per section 94(2) , if the amount of installments to be paid is less than Rs 7,500, then tax payer is not required to submit estimated return . 
since, the total amount of installments to be paid is 7,500 which is equal to 7500, so he has to submit estimated tax return.

#### (IT) Question 3 chapter 6 (page: 6.5)

 3. Mr. Jethalal Gada is running a electronic shop with annual turnover of Rs. 88 lakh in KTM municipality. Calculate tax liability if:
   i. Gross Margin was 5%?
   ii. Gross Margin was 3%?
   iii. He was engaged in electronic repairing services?

**Answer**

| Particulars | Case 1 | Amount | Case 2 | Amount | Case 3 | Amount   |
| ----------- | ------ | ------ | ------ | ------ | ------ | -------- |
| 0-30L       |        | 4,000  |        | 4,000  |        | 4,000    |
| 30L-50L     | 1%     | 20,000 | 0.25%  | 5,000  | 2%     | 40,000   |

#### (IT) Question 4 chapter 6 (page: 6.5)

 4. Mr. Hathi is a doctor and has been running a clinic in Galchhi rural municipality, Dhading with annual turnover of 82 lakh. Calculate Tax liability of Mr. Hathi.

**Answer**
Since, he is earning income from expert service to be provided by a doctor, he isn't covered u/s 4(4A) , Thus, tax has to be computed u/s 4(2) i.e. slab rate on his taxable income

#### (IT) Question 5 chapter 6 (page: 6.5)

 5. Good Services, a family run business, registered as firm sells gas cylinders to customers and is located in Kathmandu municipality. Its turnover is Rs 75 lakhs.
   Other Expenses of the firm is:
   * Cost of Cylinders sold: Rs 60,00,000
   * Salary: Rs 4,80,000
   * Telephone: Rs 60,000
   * Stationery: Rs 24,000
   * Consultancy Services: Rs 50,000
   * Other Expenses: Rs 60,000
   If the business was run by establishing company and all the income and expenses were same as mentioned above in the case of firm. Compute the tax liability of the company.
   \[Dec 2023, 5 marks]

**Answer**

Income statement

|        | Particulars            | Details   | Amount             |
| ------ | ---------------------- | --------- | ------------------ |
|        | <u>Income</u>          |           |                    |
|        | sales                  | 75,00,000 | 75,00,000          |
|        |                        |           |                    |
| -      | <u>Expense</u>         |           |                    |
|        | cost of cylinders sold | 60,00,000 |                    |
|        | Salary                 | 4,80,000  |                    |
|        | Telephone              | 60,000    |                    |
|        | stationery             | 24,000    |                    |
|        | consultancy service    | 50,000    |                    |
|        | other expense          | 60,000    | <u>(66,74,000)</u> |
|        |                        |           |                    |
| =      | Profit                 |           | 8,26,000           |
|        |                        |           |                    |
|        | sales                  |           | 75,00,000          |
| -      | cost of cylinder sold  |           | <u>(60,00,000)</u> |
|        | margin                 |           | 15,00,000          |
| $\div$ | cost of cylinder sold  |           | <u>60,00,000</u>   |
|        | profit %               |           | 25%                |

since, goods service have turnover less than 1cr and more than 30L , and profit is less than 10 lakh and more than 3 lakh and profit margin is 25% i.e. more than 3%, the goods service shall pay tax as follow

| turnover            | tax rate | tax amount    |
| ------------------- | -------- | ------------- |
| 0-30L               |          | 25,00         |
| 30-50L              | 1%       | 20,000        |
| 50L - 75L           | 0.8%     | <u>20,000</u> |
| total tax liability |          | 42,500        |

if goods service is a company, then it shall pay tax as per section 4(2) ,and tax liability shall be as follow

|          | Particulars                          | Amount     |
| -------- | ------------------------------------ | ---------- |
|          | profit                               | 8,26,000   |
| $\times$ | tax rate (schedule 1 , section 2(1)) | <u>25%</u> |
| =        | tax liability                        | 2,06,500   |

#### (IT) Question 1 chapter 7 (page: 7.10)

 1. ABC Enterprises has filed estimated tax amount of Rs 1,00,000 for the F/Y 2070/71. The proprietor of the firm had incurred Rs 15,000 up to 2071/02/29 as approved medical expenses. Find the installments amount to be paid by ABC enterprises for the FY 2070/71.

**Answer**

|          | Particulars         | Poush      | chaitra    | asadh         |
| -------- | ------------------- | ---------- | ---------- | ------------- |
|          | tax payable         | 1,00,000   | 1,00,000   | 1,00,000      |
| $\times$ | installment portion | <u>40%</u> | <u>70%</u> | <u>100%</u>   |
| =        | installment amount  | 40,000     | 70,000     | 1,00,000      |
| -        | medical tax credit  | -          | -          | 1500<br>(WN1) |
| -        | installment paid    | -          | (40,000)   | (70,000)      |
| =        | installment payable | 40,000     | 30,000     | 28,500        |

WN1: medical tax credit
lower of 
15% of 15,000 = 2,250
or 
1,500



#### (IT) Question 2 chapter 7 (page: 7.10)

 2. Details of annual estimated tax and withholding tax of A & B Co. are as follows:

| Period | Estimated Tax |
|---|---|
| Poush End | Rs 10,00,000 |
| Chaitra End | Rs 12,00,000 |
| Ashadh End | Rs 12,00,000 |
Withholding tax details:
 * Upto Poush end: Rs 20,000
 * Magh to Chaitra end: Rs 10,000
 * Chaitra to Ashadh end: Rs 15,000
Compute the total advance tax to be paid for each installments. \[June 2011 - 5 marks]

**Answer**

|          | Particulars         | Poush      | chaitra                 | asadh                         |
| -------- | ------------------- | ---------- | ----------------------- | ----------------------------- |
|          | tax payable         | 10,00,000  | 12,00,000               | 12,00,000                     |
| $\times$ | installment portion | <u>40%</u> | <u>70%</u>              | <u>100%</u>                   |
| =        | installment amount  | 4,00,000   | 8,40,000                | 12,00,000                     |
| -        | TDS                 | (20,000)   | (30,000)<br>(20k + 10k) | (45,000)<br>(20k + 10K + 15K) |
| -        | installment paid    | -          | (3,80,000)              | (8,10,000)                    |
| =        | installment payable | 3,80,000   | 4,30,000                | 3,45,000                      |


#### (IT) Question 3 chapter 7 (page: 7.10)

 3. Mr. Hari Kant Mainali is a retail trader of gas in KTM metropolitan city. He estimates his business turnover to reach around Rs. 8.5 Million during the IY 2079/80. He has no other source of income.
   The details of transactions of Mr. Mainali for IY 2079/80 as per his accounting record are as follows:
   * i. Actual Sales up to Poush 20, 2079: Rs. 3,500,000
   * ii. Estimated Sales up to Poush 30, 2079: Rs. 3,600,000
   * iii. Actual Sales up to Ashad 20, 2080: Rs. 8,400,000
   * iv. Estimated Sales for the year 2079/80: Rs. 9,000,000
Tax withheld and paid by agents during the year is as follows:
* i. Up to Poush: Rs. 10,000
* ii. From Magh to Ashad: Rs. 12,000

**Answer**
computation of installment tax payable

|     | Particulars                           | up to poush               | up to asadh     |
| --- | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------- | --------------- |
|     | actual sales up to poush 20/ asadh 20 | 35,00,000                 | 84,00,000       |
| +   | estimated sales (asadh 21-31)         | <u>                  </u> | <u>6,00,000</u> |
| =   | sales for installment                 | 35,00,000                 | 90,00,000       |
|     |                                       |                           |                 |
|     | installment tax                       |                           |                 |
| +   | 0-30L                                 | 7,500                     | 7,500           |
| +   | 30-5L/50L = 0.25%                     | 1250                      | 5,000           |
| +   | 50-90L = 0.3%                         | <u>     -     </u>        | <u>12,000</u>   |
| =   | total installment tax                 | 8,750                     | 24,500          |
| -   | installment tax paid                  | <u>   -   </u>            | <u>(8,750)</u>  |
| =   | installment tax payable               | 8,750                     | 15,750          |


#### (IT) Question 4,5 chapter 7 (page: 7.10)

 4. Mr. Jethalal is running an electronic business with a gross margin of 4% in a municipality. Details of his sales are as below:
   * i. Up to Poush 20 (Actual): Rs. 32 lakh
   * ii. Poush 21 to Poush end (Estimated): Rs. 3 lakh
   * iii. Poush 21 to Poush end (Actual): Rs. 5 lakh
   * iv. Magh 1 to Ashadh 20 (Actual): Rs. 50 lakh
   * v. Ashadh 21 to Ashadh end (Estimated): Rs. 4 lakh
   * vi. Ashadh 21 to Ashadh end (Actual): Rs. 6 lakh
Calculate estimated tax and final tax to be paid by him.

**Answer**
computation of Installment tax payable

|     | Particulars                           | up to poush               | up to asadh     |
| --- | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------- | --------------- |
|     | actual sales up to poush 20/ asadh 20 | 32,00,000                 | 87,00,000       |
| +   | estimated sales (asadh 21-31)         | <u>                  </u> | <u>4,00,000</u> |
| =   | sales for installment                 | 32,00,000                 | 91,00,000       |
|     |                                       |                           |                 |
|     | installment tax                       |                           |                 |
| +   | 0-30L                                 | 4,000                     | 4,000           |
| +   | 30-5L/50L = 1%                        | 2,000                     | 20,000          |
| +   | 50-91L = 0.8%                         | <u>     -     </u>        | <u>32,800</u>   |
| =   | total installment tax                 | 6,000                     | 56,800          |
| -   | installment tax paid                  | <u>   -   </u>            | <u>(6,000)</u>  |
| =   | installment tax payable               | 6,000                     | 50,800          |

computation of final tax payable

|     | Particulars                                 | Amount        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------- | ------------- |
|     | actual sales up to asadh end<br>(32+5+50+6) | 93,00,000     |
|     |                                             |               |
|     | tax calculation                             |               |
| +   | 0-30L                                       | 4,000         |
| +   | 30-50L = 1%                                 | 20,000        |
| +   | 50-93L = 0.8%                               | <u>34,400</u> |
| =   | total tax amount                            | 58,400        |
| -   | advance tax paid                            | <u>50,800</u> |
| =   | net tax payable                             | 7,600         |



 5. What shall be estimated tax to be paid by him if actual sales from Magh 1 to Ashadh 20 was Rs. 72 lakh and estimated expenses for the year is Rs. 92 lakh?

**Answer**
Even id his turnover was Rs 72 lakh from magh 1 to ashadh 20, the estimated tax paid by him wouldn't have been affected 
So, estimated tax on poush end = 6,000

|     | Estimated tax on ashadh end           | Amont     |
| --- | ------------------------------------- | --------- |
|     | Actual turnover up to ashad 20        | 109L      |
| +   | Estimated turnover from ashadh 21-end | <u>4L</u> |
|     | Estimated turnover for the year       | 113L      |
Since, his estimated turnover exceeds Rs 1cr, he isn't covered u/s 4(4A). Thus he needs to pay advance tax as applicable to person covered u/s 4(2)

|     |                    |              |
| --- | ------------------ | ------------ |
|     | Estimated turnover | 113L         |
| -   | Estimated expenses | <u>(92L)</u> |
|     | Estimated income   | 21L          |

computation of tax as per section 1(1) of schedule 1

| profit                   |     |               |
| ------------------------ | --- | ------------- |
| 0 - 5lakh                | 0%  | 0             |
| 5lakh - 7lakh            | 10% | 20,000        |
| 7lakh - 10lakh           | 20% | 60,000        |
| 10lakh - 20lakh          | 30% | 3,00,000      |
| 20lakh - 21lakh          | 36% | <u>36,000</u> |
| total tax payable        |     | 4,16,000      |
| (-) installment tax paid |     | (6,000)       |
| installment tax payable  |     | 4,10,000      |

Calculate the tax liabilities for the following individuals who have regular business income as below for the income year 2078/79:

| Particulars         | Mr. A         | Mr. B           | Mr. C         | Mr. D           | Mr. E           |
| :------------------ | :------------ | :-------------- | :------------ | :-------------- | :-------------- |

| Holding period      | 8 months      | 7 years         | 3 years       | 4 years         | 1 year          |

**Answer**

|     | Particulars                                         | Mr. A            | Mr. B            | Mr. C            | Mr. D            | Mr. E           |
| :-- | :-------------------------------------------------- | :--------------- | :--------------- | :--------------- | :--------------- | :-------------- |
|     | Taxable Income (Rs)                                 | 4,90,000         | 5,90,000         | 7,90,000         | 20,90,000        | 30,90,000       |

| x   | Advance tax rate u/s 95A                            | 7.5%             | 5%               | 5%               | 7.5%             | 10%             |
| =   | Total Advance tax paid u/s 95A                      | 36,750           | 24,500           | 24,500           | 36,750           | 49,000          |
|     |                                                     |                  |                  |                  |                  |                 |
|     | basic exemption limit                               | 5,00,000         | 5,00,000         | 5,00,000         | 5,00,000         | 5,00,000        |
| -   | taxable income                                      | (4,90,000)       | (5,90,000)       | (7,90,000)       | (20,90,000)      | (30,90,000)     |
| =   | unexhausted limit                                   | 10,000           | -                | -                | -                | -               |
|     |                                                     |                  |                  |                  |                  |                 |
|     | Taxable Income (Rs)                                 | 4,90,000         | 5,90,000         | 7,90,000         | 20,90,000        | 30,90,000       |
|     | 0-5L = 1%/0%                                        | 0                | 0                | 5,000            | 5,000            | 5,000           |
|     | 5L-0/5.9/7L = 10%                                   | -                | 9,000            | 20,000           | 20,000           | 20,000          |
|     | 7L-0/0/7.9/10L = 20%                                | -                | -                | 60,000           | 60,000           | 60,000          |
|     | 10L-20L = 30%                                       | -                | -                | -                | 3,00,000         | 3,00,000        |
|     | 20L-0/0/0/20.9/30.9L = 36%                          | <u>    -    </u> | <u>    -    </u> | <u>    -    </u> | <u>    -    </u> | <u>4,25,100</u> |
|     | tax liability u/s 1(1) of schedule 1                | 0                | 9,000            | 85,000           | 3,85,000         | 8,10,100        |
| +   | Tax on NBCA                                         | <u>36,750</u>    | <u>24,500</u>    | <u>24,500</u>    | <u>36,750</u>    | <u>49,000</u>   |
| =   | Total tax liability                                 | 36,750           | 33,500           | 1,09,500         | 4,21,750         | 8,59,100        |
| -   | unexhausted limit $\times$ Advance tax rate u/s 95A | (750)            | -                | -                | -                | -               |
| -   | tax credit of advance tax paid                      | <u>(36,750)</u>  | <u>(24,500)</u>  | <u>(24,500)</u>  | <u>(36,750)</u>  | <u>(49,000)</u> |
| =   | Net tax laibility                                   | (750)            | 9,000            | 85,000           | 3,85,000         | 8,10,100        |

#### (IT) Question 5 chapter 8 (page: 8.13)

5. Business Income & Land Sales
Find the tax liability of Mr. Ram Narayan who has the following income during F/Y 2079/80:
 * Business Income: Rs. 1,900,000
 * Income from sales of Land: Rs. 1,000,000

**Answer**

|     | Particulars              |                 |
| --- | ------------------------ | --------------- |
|     | income from sale of land | 10,00,000       |
| x   | Advance tax rate u/s 95A | <u>5%</u>       |
| =   | Advance tax u/s 95A      | 50,000          |
|     |                          |                 |
|     | Business income          | 19,00,000       |
|     | 0 - 5L = 0%              | 0               |
|     | 5L - 7L = 10%            | 20,000          |
|     | 7L - 10L = 20%           | 60,000          |
|     | 10L - 19L = 30%          | <u>2,70,000</u> |
|     | tax liability            | 3,50,000        |
| +   | Advance tax u/s 95A      | <u>50,000</u>   |
| =   | Total tax liability      | 4,00,000        |
| -   | advance tax paid         | <u>(50,000)</u> |
| =   | Net tax liability        | 4,00,000        |

#### (IT) Question 9,10 chapter 8 (page: 8.13)

9. Foreign Income and Tax Paid
Ram has a source of income in Nepal and also in more than one foreign country. During the year 78-79, his income and tax paid in each foreign country are given below:

| Country   | Income (Rs)  | Tax Paid (Rs) |
| :-------- | :----------- | :------------ |
| Australia | Rs. 2,00,000 | Rs. 60,000    |
| USA       | Rs. 1,50,000 | Rs. 30,000    |
| UAE       | Rs. 1,00,000 | Rs. -         |
| Nepal     | Rs. 6,50,000 | Rs. 5,000     |
Ram is a resident natural person and has opted for couple. Calculate his tax liability. (June 2012, 5 Marks - ICAN)

**Answer**

| Country   | income       | Actual tax paid + <br>PY carry forward | Average tax | tax credit allowed | amount carried forward |
| --------- | ------------ | -------------------------------------- | ----------- | ------------------ | ---------------------- |
| Australia | Rs. 2,00,000 | Rs. 60,000                             |             |                    |                        |
| USA       | Rs. 1,50,000 | Rs. 30,000                             |             |                    |                        |
| UAE       | Rs. 1,00,000 | Rs. -                                  |             |                    |                        |

working note 1
calculation of average tax rate

|        | Particulars           | Australia       | USA             | UAE             |
| ------ | --------------------- | --------------- | --------------- | --------------- |
|        | 0-5L = 1%             | 5,000           | 5,000           | 5,000           |
|        | 5L-7L = 10%           | 20,000          | 20,000          | 20,000          |
|        | 7L-8.5L/8L/7.5L = 20% | <u>30,000</u>   | <u>20,000</u>   | <u>10,000</u>   |
|        | Total tax             | 55,000          | 45,000          | 35,000          |
| $\div$ | total income          | <u>8,50,000</u> | <u>8,00,000</u> | <u>7,50,000</u> |
| =      | average tax rate      | 6.47            |                 |                 |
| x      | foreign income        | <u>2,00,000</u> | <u>1,50,000</u> | <u>1,00,000</u> |
| =      | Average tax           | 12,941          | 8,437.5         | 4,666.67        |

#### (IT) Question 11 chapter 8 (page: 8.13)

11. Resident Status and Tax Set-off
Mr. A receives Royalty of Rs. 25 lakh from Nepal on which 15% tax was deducted at source. Mr. A occasionally visits Nepal and during the year 20X-59/X-060 he stays in Nepal for 100 days. He is a resident of USA and he has to pay tax in USA. Can he claim the tax he has to pay in USA to be set off against tax deductible in Nepal and claim refund as that is the only income that he receives in Nepal? He has to pay a tax at 35% in the USA in the Royalty income earned. (Dec 2004 - 5 Marks)

**Answer**
According to provision of income tax Act 2058, A natural person is said to be resident of Nepal if any of the following conditions are satisfied:
a. His habitual place of abode is in Nepal, or,
b. He stays in Nepal for $\geq$ 183 days during the income year, or
c. He is deputed abroad by GON

In the given case, none of the above case is satisfied. Thus he is said to be non-resident person

Further section 71 states that resident person may claim foreign tax credit for tax paid in foreign country
Also section 6 states that assessable income of a non resident person includes income earned by the person having source in Nepal only
Thus, in the given case, only royalty income is taxable for Mr A which attracts 15% final withholding tax and hence the question of him getting foreign tax credit for tax paid in USA does not arise

Question 2
Mr. Bhai Raja is a person working in a Financial Institution as chief manager. Besides working in a financial institution, he is a professor of accounts in Shankar Dev Campus. He generates the following income for the year 2067/68:
I. From Financial Institution:
 * Monthly Remuneration: Rs. 40,000
 * Dashain Allowance: Rs. 40,000
 * Education Allowance: Rs. 2,000/month
 * Expenditure Allowance: Rs. 2,000/month
 * Bonus: Rs. 60,000
 * Remuneration from teaching in Shankar Dev Campus: Rs. 6,000/month.
II. He is provided a car along with a driver for his official and personal purpose. Monthly remuneration and allowance of the driver is Rs. 8,000.
III. Petrol Expenditure is provided for his car which is Rs. 10,000 per month and maintenance expenditure of such car is Rs. 25,000 in Fiscal Year 2067/68.
IV. He is provided a housing facility by the employer. Monthly expenditure of telephone connected in his resident is Rs. 2,000/month. Out of this 50% is his personal telephone expenditure. A security guard is being provided in his resident and it is being paid Rs. 6,000/month for the use of security guard in his resident, Rs. 3000 being deducted from his monthly salary.
V. He has used the loan amounting to Rs. 10 lakhs under housing loan facility at the rate of 5% interest. 8% interest rate is being charged by the institution under similar loan to other borrowers.
VI. He has received one month salary against his house leave during Mangsir, 2067.
VII. The employer contributes 10% of his drawn salary and the employee same amount from his salary, to approved provident fund. Also, the employer contributes Rs. 20,000 per month to Citizen Investment Fund upon request of employee.
VIII. He has covered the insurance of Rs. 250,000 and Rs. 150,000 of his wife in Rastriya Beema Sansthan. He pays the premium of Rs. 19,000 and Rs. 14,000 respectively in that Fiscal Year. Also, he has contributed to tax exempted entity approved from department as donation amounting to Rs. 60,000.
IX. He has expended out Rs. 20,000 for his treatment in Teaching Hospital.
X. He and his wife declared that they are a couple in the Fiscal Year 2067/68 and his wife does not have any income in that Fiscal Year.
Required: Assessable Income, Taxable Income and Tax liability of Mr. Bhai Raja for the Fiscal Year 067/68.

#### (IT) Question 1 chapter 10 (page: 10.19)

Question 1
Rambilas Private Limited obtained a loan of Rs. 1 crore @ 12% p.a. on Shrawan 1, 2079 for construction of a building. The building was ready/put to use on Poush end, 2080.
How is the interest treated as per accounting standards and tax laws?

**Answer**

| Date    | Particulars                     | expense                           | capitalize                           |
| ------- | ------------------------------- | --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ |
|         | **As per accounting standards** |                                   |                                      |
| 2079/80 | interest 12L                    | -                                 | 12L                                  |
| 2080/81 | interest 12L                    | 6L (6/12 x 12L)<br>(magh - asadh) | 6L (6/12 x 12L)<br>(shrawan - poush) |
|         | **Total**                       | **6L**                            | **18L**                              |
|         |                                 |                                   |                                      |
|         | **As per tax laws**             |                                   |                                      |
| 2079/80 | interest 12L                    | -                                 | 12L                                  |
| 2080/81 | interest 12L                    | 12L                               | -                                    |
|         | **Total**                       | **12L**                           | **12L**                              |




#### (IT) Question 2 chapter 10 (page: 10.19)

Question 2
M/s ABC Construction Company Pvt. Ltd., a construction company, has purchased a heavy duty earth moving machine from M/s XYZ Company Pvt. Ltd. with the amount payable in five installments which are payable at the beginning of the year. The amount of each installment is Rs. 10 lakhs. Calculate the amount of interest to be claimed under Section 14 of the act by showing the year wise interest and repayment of debt claim. Rate of Interest is 12%.
What if the installments are to be paid at the end of each year?

**Answer**


 Market Value of the Machine
 
* **Cash Price Calculation:** 
	* $\text{Cash Price} = 10,00,000 \times [1 + \text{PVIFA}(12\%, 4 \text{ yrs})]$
	* $\text{Cash Price} = 10,00,000 \times 4.037349$
*  **Market Value = Rs. 40,37,349**

**Statement Showing Year-Wise Interest (Case 1)**
*Installments paid at the beginning of each year (Annuity Due).*

| Yr | Opening Balance | Installment | Interest (12%) | Principal Repay | Closing Balance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **0** | 40,37,349 | 10,00,000 | — | 10,00,000 | 30,37,349 |
| **1** | 30,37,349 | 10,00,000 | 364,482 | 635,518 | 24,01,831 |
| **2** | 24,01,831 | 10,00,000 | 288,220 | 711,780 | 16,90,051 |
| **3** | 16,90,051 | 10,00,000 | 202,806 | 797,194 | 892,857 |
| **4** | 892,857 | 10,00,000 | 107,143 | 892,857 | — |
> **Summary:** The interest allowed shall be **Rs. 364,482; 288,220; 202,806; and 107,143** in respective years.
> 

**Case 2: If installments are to be paid at the end of each year**
*Calculation for Ordinary Annuity.*
* **Market Value Calculation:**
	* $\text{Market Value} = 10 \text{ Lakh} \times [\text{PVIFA}(12\%, 5 \text{ yrs})]$
	* $\text{Market Value} = 10,00,000 \times 3.604776$
* Market Value = Rs. 36,04,776**

**Statement Showing Year-Wise Interest (Case 2)**

| Yr | Opening Balance | Installment | Interest (12%) | Principal Repay | Closing Balance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | 36,04,776 | 10,00,000 | 432,573 | 567,427 | 30,37,349 |
| **2** | 30,37,349 | 10,00,000 | 364,482 | 635,518 | 24,01,831 |
| **3** | 24,01,831 | 10,00,000 | 288,220 | 711,780 | 16,90,051 |
| **4** | 16,90,051 | 10,00,000 | 202,806 | 797,194 | 892,857 |
| **5** | 892,857 | 10,00,000 | 107,143 | 892,857 | — |





#### (IT) Question 3 chapter 10 (page: 10.19)

Question 3
The P/L account of XYZ Ltd (Controlled by Mr. James (NR)) is given below:

| Particulars | Amount (Rs.) |
|---|---|
| Sales | 10,00,000 |
| Interest Income | 40,000 |
| Total Income | 10,40,000 |
| Less: Admin Expenses | 6,00,000 |
| Less: Interest Expenses (Rs. 60,000 is payable to Mr. James) | 2,00,000 |
| Less: Depreciation | 2,00,000 |
| Total expenses | 10,00,000 |
| Net profit | 40,000 |
Required: Amount of interest allowed.

WN1
calculation of ATI(Adjusted taxable income)

|     | Particulars                       | Detail    | Amount                             |
| --- | --------------------------------- | --------- | ---------------------------------- |
|     | Sales                             | 10,00,000 |                                    |
|     | **Total income**                  |           | **10,00,000**                      |
|     |                                   |           |                                    |
| -   | Expense                           |           |                                    |
|     | admin expense                     | 6,00,000  |                                    |
|     | depriciation                      | 2,00,000  |                                    |
|     | **Total expense**                 |           | <u><strong>(8,00,000)</strong></u> |
|     |                                   |           |                                    |
| =   | **Adjusted taxable income (ATI)** |           | **2,00,000**                       |


|     | interest claim= lower of                                          |                                                    |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
|     | 50% of ATI                                                        | 1,00,000                                           |
| +   | Actual interest income                                            | <u>40,000</u>                                      |
|     |                                                                   | 1,40,000                                           |
|     |                                                                   | or                                                 |
|     | <span style="color:rgb(192, 0, 0)">Actual interest expense</span> | <span style="color:rgb(192, 0, 0)">2,00,000</span> |

Hence, interest expense allowed to deduct as per section 14(2) will be 1,40,000

>[!warning]
>while calculating ATI **Actual interest paid to controlling entity** is used 
>interest u/s 14(1) =  (interest expense - interest paid to controlling entity)


|     | interest claim= lower of                    |               |
| --- | ------------------------------------------- | ------------- |
|     | 50% of ATI                                  | 1,00,000      |
| +   | Actual interest income                      | <u>40,000</u> |
|     |                                             | 1,40,000      |
|     |                                             | or            |
|     | interest expense paid to controlling entity | 60,000        |

|     |                                                                                |          |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------- |
|     | Interest u/s 14(1)<br>(interest expense - interest paid to controlling entity) | 1,40,000 |
|     | interest u/s 14(2)                                                             | 60,000   |

From the following P/L account of an exempt controlled resident entity, compute the taxable income for the year:

| Particulars | Amount (Rs.) |
|---|---|

| Total Income | 7,55,000 |

| Interest Expenses | 75,000 |
| Depreciation Expenses | 20,000 |
| Total Expenses | 7,15,000 |
| Net Profit | 40,000 |

**Answer**
