| --------------------- | ------------ | ----------------- |
| Sales                 | 7,50,000     |                   |
| **Total Income**      |              | 7,50,000          |
| Cost of Sales         | 5,00,000     |                   |
| Administrative        | 1,20,000     |                   |
| Depreciation Expenses | 20,000       |                   |
| Total Expenses        |              | <u>(6,40,000)</u> |
| ATI                   |              | 1,10,000          |

|     | interest claim= lower of                    |              |
| --- | ------------------------------------------- | ------------ |
|     | 50% of ATI                                  | 55,000       |
| +   | Actual interest income                      | <u>5,000</u> |
|     |                                             | 60,000       |
|     |                                             | or           |
|     | interest expense paid to controlling entity | 75,000       |

|     |                                                                                |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------ |
|     | Interest u/s 14(1)<br>(interest expense - interest paid to controlling entity) | 0      |
|     | interest u/s 14(2)                                                             | 60,000 |

>[!tip]
>all interest paid is interest paid to controlling entity 
>why?
>sir le tei garnu vako xa, lecture 52 time 1:18:50


**Computation of taxable income**

| Particulars           | Amount (Rs.) | Amount (Rs.)    |
| --------------------- | ------------ | --------------- |
| Sales                 | 7,50,000     |                 |
| Interest Income       | 5,000        |                 |
| Total Income          |              | 7,55,000        |
| Cost of Sales         | 5,00,000     |                 |
| Administrative        | 1,20,000     |                 |
| Interest Expenses     | 60,000       |                 |
| Depreciation Expenses | 20,000       |                 |
| Total Expenses        |              | <u>7,00,000</u> |
| Taxable income        |              | 55,000          |






#### (IT) Question 5 chapter 10 (page: 10.19)

Question 5
MX enterprises has the following transaction during the year. Find deductible cost of sales for the income year.
 * Opening stock: Rs. 50,000 (FIFO)
 * Purchased during the year: Rs. 5,00,000
 * Sales during the year (GP ratio 25%): Rs. 5,00,000
 * Value of closing stock (Market Value):
   * Case A: Rs. 1,20,000
   * Case B: Rs. 1,50,000
   * Case C: Rs. 2,00,000
   * Case D: Rs. Nil

**Answer**

sales = 5,00,000
cogs = 75% of 5,00,000
opening + purchase - closing stock = 3,75,000
50,000 + 5,00,000 - closing stock = 3,75,000
$\therefore$ closing stock = 1,75,000

>[!tip] direct expense is irrelevant / zero

value of closing stock (lower of cost or NRV)

|     | Particulars             | case A          | case B          | case C          | case D   |
| --- | ----------------------- | --------------- | --------------- | --------------- | -------- |
|     | cost                    | 1,75,000        | 1,75,000        | 1,75,000        | 1,75,000 |
|     | NRV                     | <u>1,20,000</u> | <u>1,50,000</u> | <u>2,00,000</u> | <u>0</u> |
|     | **closing stock value** | **1,20,000**    | **1,50,000**    | **1,75,000**    | **0**    |
|     |                         |                 |                 |                 |          |
|     | purchase                | 5,00,000        | 5,00,000        | 5,00,000        | 5,00,000 |
| +   | opening stock           | 50,000          | 50,000          | 50,000          | 50,000   |
| -   | closing stock           | <u>1,20,000</u> | <u>1,50,000</u> | <u>1,75,000</u> | <u>0</u> |
|     | cost of sale deductible | 4,30,000        | 4,00,000        | 3,75,000        | 5,50,000 |



#### (IT) Question 6 chapter 10 (page: 10.20)

Question 6
Simple Innovations Pvt. Ltd., a manufacturing company received a tax assessment order for fiscal year 20X-66/X-67 on 20X-69/8/20 from Inland Revenue Office. Some of the issues stated in the assessment order are as follows:
a. Company has capitalized interest cost worth Rs. 19,34,569 (interest till X67.01.10, on loan taken for machinery) to plant and machinery ready to/put to use in 20X 67.01.10.
b. Value of opening stock has included Repair & Maintenance Expense of Rs. 7,56,760 and Closing Stock includes Repair Expense Rs. 6,54,650.
Management is not satisfied with the assessment order and seeks your consultancy.

**Answer**
a.
As per section 14(1) of income tax act 2058, interest expenses on loan taken to acquire assets is allowed as deduction if the asset has been put to use during the year 
It means that interest for the whole year is allowed as deduction even if the acid was put to use for a single day 
In the given case company has capitalized interest till the date when the asset is ready/put to use
Thus, The contention of the company is incorrect . It shall claim interest expense of Rs19,34,569 as deduction under section 14 as the assets was put to use during the year.

b.
As per section 15 of income tax act 2058, cost of stock in trade or cost of trading asset includes overhead cost of factory, while computing cost as per consumption cost method. 
However, it further states that overhead cost of factory shall not include depreciation and repair expenses incurred in factory.
In the given case, simple innovations Private limited has included repair cost in opening and closing stock which is incorrect in line with the above provision

#### (IT) Question 7 chapter 10 (page: 10.20)

Question 7
The status of property, plant and equipment and repair and improvement expenditure of XYZ and Co. during the income year 2068-2069 is as follows:

| Block of Assets  | Repair and Improvement (Rs.) | Depreciation Basis (Rs.) |
| ---------------- | ---------------------------- | ------------------------ |
| Building Block   | 20,00,000                    | 10,00,00,000             |
| Computer block   | 1,00,000                     | 8,00,000                 |
| Automobile block | 16,00,000                    | 1,60,00,000              |
| Total            | 37,00,000                    | 11,68,00,000             |
i. Can repair and improvement expenditure of Rs. 37,00,000 be claimed? If not compute the amount of repair and improvement expenditure that can be claimed.
ii. What is the implication of the amount of repair and improvement if any, which cannot be claimed during the income year 68/69?

**Answer**


| Particulars              | Building block | Computer block | Automobile block |
| ------------------------ | -------------- | -------------- | ---------------- |
| Depreciable basis        | 10,00,00,000   | 8,00,000       | 1,60,00,000      |
| 7% of depreciable basis  | 70,00,000      | 56,000         | 11,20,000        |
| Actual repair expense    | 20,00,000      | 1,00,000       | 16,00,000        |
| Repair expense allowed   | 20,00,000      | 56,000         | 11,20,000        |
| Repair expense unallowed | -              | 44,000         | 4,80,000         |

Total repair expenses allowed = Rs 31,76,000

B.
Rs 44,000 and Rs 4,80,000 salvi added to opening depreciation base of computer and automobile block in next year respectively

Question 8
Saphal byabasai P. Ltd. is a company having following assets details under Block - D.

 * Additions:
   * Up to Poush end: Rs. 25 lakh (Machine B)

   * Baishakh to Ashadh end: Rs. 18 lakh (Machine D)
 * Disposal proceeds:
   * Case i: Rs. 60 lakhs (Machine A & C)
   * Case ii: Rs. 100 lakhs (Machine A & C)
   * Case iii: Rs. 70 lakhs (Machine A, B, C & D)
   * Case iv: Rs. 120 lakhs (Machine A, B, C & D)
The company incurred repair expenses of Rs. 5 lakhs during the income year.

**Answer**

|     | Particulars                                          | case i            | case ii          | case iii         | case iv            |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------- | ----------------- | ---------------- | ---------------- | ------------------ |
|     | opening                                              | 42L               | 42L              | 42L              | 42L                |
| +   | period wise purchase (WN1)<br>(for partial disposal) | 47L               | 47L              | -                | -                  |
| +   | purchase <br>(for full disposal)                     | -                 | -                | 67L              | 67L                |
| -   | Disposal                                             | <u>(60L)</u>      | <u>(100L)</u>    | <u>(70L)</u>     | <u>(120L)</u>      |
| =   | net value                                            | 29L               | (11L)            | 39L              | (11L)              |
|     | classification                                       | Depreciable bases | Balancing charge | Balancing charge | Terminal value     |
|     | Treatment                                            |                   | goes to income   | goes to income   | allowed as expense |

**case i**

|     |                      |            |
| --- | -------------------- | ---------- |
|     | Depreciable base     | 29L        |
| x   | Depreciation rate    | <u>15%</u> |
| =   | Depreciation allowed | 4.35L      |
**Calculation of closing WDV**


|     | Particulars                      | case i         | case ii | case iii | case iv |
| --- | -------------------------------- | -------------- | ------- | -------- | ------- |
|     | Depreciable basis                | 29L            | -       | -        | -       |
| -   | Depreciation                     | (4.35L)        |         |          |         |
| +   | period wise purchase unabsorbed  | 20L            |         |          |         |
| +   | unabsorbed R&M  (WN2)            | 2.97L          |         |          |         |
| +   | unallowed PCC (for block D only) | -              |         |          |         |
| +   | unallowed R&D (for block D only) | <u>-      </u> |         |          |         |
| =   | closing value of block           | 47.62L         |         |          |         |


WN1
calculation of period wise purchse

| date                  | purchase   | allowed                | unallowed  |
| --------------------- | ---------- | ---------------------- | ---------- |
| up to poush           | 25L        | 25L                    | 0          |
| magh to chaitra end   | 24L        | 2/3 of 24L = 16L       | 8L         |
| baisakh to ashadh end | <u>18L</u> | <u>1/3 of 18L = 6L</u> | <u>12L</u> |
| total                 | 67L        | 47L                    | 20L        |

**WN2**

|     | Particulars                    | case i         | case ii | case iii | case iv |
| --- | ------------------------------ | -------------- | ------- | -------- | ------- |
|     | Depreciable basis              | 29L            | -       | -        | -       |
| A   | 7% of depreciable basis        | 2.03L          |         |          |         |
| B   | Actual R&M                     | <u>5L</u>      |         |          |         |
| =   | R&M allowed (lower of A and B) | 2.03L          |         |          |         |
|     |                                |                |         |          |         |
|     | Actual R&M                     | 5L             |         |          |         |
| -   | R&M allowed                    | <u>(2.03L)</u> |         |          |         |
| =   | Unallowed/unabsorbed R&M       | 2.97L          |         |          |         |

#### (IT) Question 15 chapter 10 (page: 10.22)

Question 15
Fulkumari private Limited purchase a software costing Rs 6 lakhs on Magh 15, 2079. Estimated life of the software is 5 years and 2 months.
Calculated the available depreciation on the said software from 79-80 to 84-85.

**Answer**

Since the useful life is approximately **5 years**, the straight-line depreciation rate is calculated as:
**Initial Allocation (Income Tax Context):**
 * **Purchased in Magh (Mid-year):** Based on the 2/3 rule for the year of purchase.
 * **Initial Capitalization:** $6,00,000 \times \frac{2}{3} = 4,00,000$
 * **Unabsorbed Addition:** $6,00,000 \times \frac{1}{3} = 2,00,000$ (added to base in the subsequent year)

**Computation of Depreciation Table (79-80 to 84-85)**

| Particulars | 79-80 | 80-81 | 81-82 | 82-83 | 83-84 | 84-85 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Opening Balance** | — | 5,20,000 | 4,00,000 | 2,80,000 | 1,60,000 | 40,000 |
| **(+) Purchase (6L \times 2/3)** | 4,00,000 | — | — | — | — | — |
| **Depreciation Base** | **4,00,000** | **5,20,000** | **4,00,000** | **2,80,000** | **1,60,000** | **40,000** |
| **(-) Depⁿ @ 20%** | (80,000) | (1,20,000) | (1,20,000) | (1,20,000) | (1,20,000) | (40,000)* |
| **(+) Unabsorbed Addition** | 2,00,000 | — | — | — | — | — |
| **Closing Base for Next Year** | **5,20,000** | **4,00,000** | **2,80,000** | **1,60,000** | **40,000** | **—** |
> [!note]
> In the final year (84-85), the depreciation is limited to the remaining book value of 40,000 to bring the asset value to zero.

#### (IT) Question 22 chapter 10 (page: 10.23)


Question 22
Fewa Pvt Ltd has been established by Nepalese promoters under foreign direct investment. The eighty percent of the total capital has been held by a Non-Resident persons. In addition, The Pvt limited has borrowed the amount from foreign investors. For the year ended Ashad end, 2070, the profit and loss account of the company has the following transactions.

| Particulars | Amount (Rs) |
|---|---|
| Sales | Rs 50,00,000 |
| Interest Income | Rs 75,000 |
| Total Income | Rs 50,75,000 |
| General Expenses | Rs 20,00,000 |
| Admin Exp | Rs 11,00,000 |
| Interest Exp. | Rs 15,00,000 |
| Pollution Control Expenses | Rs 3,00,000 |
| Total Expenses | Rs 49,00,000 |
| Net Profit | Rs 1,75,000 |
i. Interest expenses Rs 14,00,000 out of total has been charged on the borrowed amount from foreign investors. Answer whether all the above expenses can be claimed under Income Tax Act, 2058?
ii. If these expenses cannot be claimed during the year, what would be the implication?
iii. Compute taxable income.

**Answer** 

 **1. Computation of Adjusted Taxable Income (ATI)**
To calculate the limits for Interest (u/s 14) and Pollution Control Cost (u/s 17), we first determine the ATI.

| Particulars | Sec. 14 (Interest) | Sec. 17 (PCC) |
|---|---|---|
| **Inclusion u/s 7(2)** |  |  |
| Sales | 5,000,000 | 5,000,000 |
| Interest Income | — | 75,000 |
| **Less: Deductions** |  |  |
| General Ded. u/s 13 (Gen + Admin) | (3,100,000) | (3,100,000) |
| Interest Exp. u/s 14(1) | — | (100,000) |
| Interest Exp. u/s 14(2) | — | (14,000,000)* |
| Pollution Control Exp u/s 17 | (300,000) | — |
| **Adjusted Taxable Income (ATI)** | **1,600,000** | **1,475,000** |



 **2. Computation of Allowable Deductions**

 
 **A. Interest Allowed u/s 14(2)**
Applicable to interest paid to controlling entities (Non-Residents). Limit is the lower of:
 1. **50% of ATI + Interest Income:** (50% of 1,600,000) + 75,000 = **Rs 875,000**
 2. **Actual Interest u/s 14(2):** **Rs 1,400,000**
**Allowable Interest:** Rs 875,000

Out of interest paid to NR promoters, only Rs 8,75,000 shall be allowed. Remaining 5,25,000 shall be carried forward and added to interest paid for the purpose of computation of limit u/s 14(2)



**B. Pollution Control Cost (PCC) u/s 17**
Limit is the lower of:
 1. **50% of ATI:** 50% of 1,475,000 = **Rs 237,500**
 2. **Actual Expenses:** **Rs 300,000**
 **Allowable PCC:** Rs 237,500

 **Implication:** The unallowed PCC (Rs 62,500) shall be added to the opening depreciation base of **Block D** in the next year.


 
 
 **3. Computation of Taxable Income**
 
| Particulars                                    | Note            | Amount (Rs)      |
| ---------------------------------------------- | --------------- | ---------------- |
| **Inclusions u/s 7(2)**                        |                 |                  |
| Sales                                          |                 | 5,000,000        |
| Interest Income                                |                 | <u>75,000</u>    |
| **Total Inclusions**                           |                 | **5,075,000**    |
|                                                |                 |                  |
| **Less: Deductions**                           |                 |                  |
| i) General Ded. u/s 13 (2,000,000 + 1,100,000) |                 | (3,100,000)      |
| ii) Interest Exp. u/s 14(1)                    |                 | (100,000)        |
| iii) Interest Exp. u/s 14(2)                   | (Limit applied) | (875,000)        |
| iv) PCC u/s 17                                 | (Limit applied) | <u>(237,500)</u> |
|                                                |                 |                  |
| **Taxable Income**                             |                 | **762,500**      |





#### (IT) Question 23 chapter 10 (page: 10.23)

From the following details of ABC P.Ltd. calculate taxable income for the income year 2079/80 from following details

| Particulars                                                   | Amount (Rs.) |
| ------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------ |
| Sales                                                         | 9,000        |
| Other Income                                                  | 50           |
| Interest Income (Business related)                            | 100          |
| Investment Income                                             | 100          |
| **Expenses / Deductions:**                                    |              |
| Cost of Sales (Trading Assets)                                | 5,000        |
| Administrative Expenses                                       | 500          |
| Donation                                                      | 100          |
| Interest expenses paid to exempt controlling person u/s 14(2) | 300          |
| Pollution control expenses u/s 17                             | 1,500        |
| Research and Development expenses u/s 18                      | 1,000        |
| Depreciation                                                  | 50           |
| Investment income                                             | 100          |

**Answer**

Statement of Taxable Income
**For the Income Year 2079-80**

|        | Particulars                              | Note | Amount (Rs.) | Amount (Rs.) |
| ------ | ---------------------------------------- | ---- | ------------ | ------------ |
| **A)** | **Income from Investment**               |      | **100**      | **100**      |
|        |                                          |      |              |              |
| **B)** | **Income from Business**                 |      |              |              |
|        | <u>Inclusions u/s 7(2):</u>              |      |              |              |
|        | * Sales                                  |      | 9,000        |              |
|        | * Interest Income                        |      | 100          |              |
|        | * Other Income                           |      | 50           |              |
|        | **Gross Business Income**                |      | **9,150**    |              |
|        |                                          |      |              |              |
|        | <u>Less: Deductions</u>                  |      |              |              |
|        | i) General Deduction u/s 13 (Admin Exp.) |      | (500)        |              |
|        | ii) Interest Exp. u/s 14(2)              | 2    | (300)        |              |
|        | iii) Cost of Trading Assets u/s 15       |      | (5,000)      |              |
|        | iv) Pollution Control Cost u/s 17        | 3    | (1,150)      |              |
|        | v) Research & Development Cost u/s 18    | 3    | (900)        |              |
|        | vi) Depreciation u/s 19                  |      | <u>(50)</u>  |              |
| A + B  | **Adjusted Business Income**             |      | **1,250**    | **1,250**    |
|        |                                          |      |              |              |
| **C)** | **Reductions**                           |      |              |              |
|        | i) Donation to tax exempt entity u/s 12  | 4    | **(45)**     | **(45)**     |
|        |                                          |      |              |              |
|        | **D) Taxable Income (A + B - C)**        |      |              | **1,305**    |

<u>Working Notes</u>

1. Computation of Adjusted Taxable Income (ATI)

| Particulars            | for sec. 12 | for sec. 14(2) | for sec. 17 | for sec. 18 |
| ---------------------- | ----------- | -------------- | ----------- | ----------- |
| Income from investment | 100         | 100            | X           | X           |
| Income from Business   | 9,150       | 9,050*         | 9,150       | 9,150       |
| **Less: Deductions**   |             |                |             |             |
| u/s 13                 | (500)       | (500)          | (500)       | (500)       |
| u/s 14                 | (300)       | X              | (300)       | (300)       |
| u/s 15                 | (5,000)     | (5,000)        | (5,000)     | (5,000)     |
| u/s 17                 | (1,500)     | (1,500)        | X           | (1,500)     |
| u/s 18                 | (1,000)     | (1,000)        | (1,000)     | X           |
| u/s 19                 | (50)        | (50)           | (50)        | (50)        |
|                        |             |                |             |             |
| <u>Reduction</u>       |             |                |             |             |
| Donation u/s 12        | x           | x              | x           | x           |
| **ATI**                | **900**     | **1,100**      | **2,300**   | **1,800**   |
> **Note:** For Sec 14(2), interest income is excluded from the business inclusion line in these workings (9,150 - 100 = 9,050).
> 

2. Allowable Interest Expenses u/s 14(2)
	Lower of:
		50% of ATI + interest income = 550+100 = **650**
	    Actual expenses = **300**
 $\therefore$ **Allowable = 300**


3. Allowable Expenses u/s 17 & 18**

| Particulars          | Sec. 17 (PCC)        | Sec. 18 (R&D)      |
| -------------------- | -------------------- | ------------------ |
| Lower of             |                      |                    |
| i) 50% of ATI        | 1,150 (50% of 2,300) | 900 (50% of 1,800) |
| ii) Actual Expenses  | 1,500                | 1,000              |
| **Allowable Amount** | **1,150**            | **900**            |



**4. Allowable Donation u/s 12**
	Lower of:
		5% of ATI  = **45**
	    Actual expenses = **100**
	    limit = **100**
 $\therefore$ **Allowable = 45**





#### (IT) Question 24 chapter 10 (page: 10.24)

Question 24
X & Co., a sole trading firm owned by Mr. X, dealing with the business of selling male garments had a profit of Rs. 1,200,000 in FY 2071/72. During the same financial year, X & Co. has opened up another shop at different location for selling exclusively female garments. The outlet being a new, it has suffered a loss of Rs. 200,000. Mr. X had registered another proprietorship X & Sons for furniture business. It incurred a loss of Rs. 300,000. Also, he has a partnership firm with his spouse that too incurred a loss of Rs. 200,000. For income Tax purpose, Mr. X prepared the financial statement by clubbing income statements of all firms showing profit of Rs. 500,000. Is the clubbing done by Mr. X justified?

**Answer**

According to **Section 20 of Income Tax Act, 2058**, a person may compute income from business by deducting unrelieved loss of:
i) The year incurred by the person from **any other business**.
ii) Previous **7 income years** incurred by the person from any business.

Further, as per the provisions of the Act, a person is classified into **Natural Person/Individual** and **Entity**.
 * **Individual:** Means an individual and also includes a **proprietorship** owned by an individual. It means the individual and its proprietorship firm are treated as the **same person**.
 * **Partnership Firm:** Being an entity, it is **different from its partners**. Thus, partners and partnership firms are treated as separate persons.
 
In the given case, **Mr. X** has two proprietorship firms:
 1. **X & Co.:** Selling male and female garments.
 2. **X & Sons:** Dealing with furniture.
Since these are treated as the same person, **loss set-off is allowed** between them.
**However**, a partnership firm is a separate person. Thus, loss incurred by a partnership firm **cannot** be set off against income earned in a proprietorship firm.

Hence, the taxable income of the proprietorship is computed as follows:

| Particulars | Amount (in Lakh) |
|---|---|
| Income from sale of male garments | 12 lakh |
| (-) Loss from sale of female garments | (2 lakh) |
| (-) Loss in X & Sons | (3 lakh) |
| **Taxable Income** | **7 lakh** |
However, loss incurred in a partnership firm may be set off with business/investment income till the subsequent 7 years within that specific entity.




#### (IT) Question 25 chapter 10 (page: 10.24)

Question 25
Utsab & Co., a proprietorship firm has purchased a car of Rs. 20 lakhs on 15th Shrawan, 2079 and incurred repair expenses of Rs. 2 lakh during the income year.
Compute allowable depreciation and repair for the income year 2079-80 and 2080-81 if it was used for personal purpose (50%) in both the year.

**Answer** 

**Computation of Allowable Depreciation & Repair**

| Particulars | 2079-80 (Rs.) | 2080-81 (Rs.) |
|---|---|---|
| Opening Depreciation Base | — | 16.6 lakh |
| (+) Additions | 20 lakh | — |
| **Depreciation Base** | **20 lakh** | **16.6 lakh** |
| (-) Depreciation @ 20% | (4 lakh) | (3.32 lakh) |
| **Balance** | **16 lakh** | **13.28 lakh** |
| (+) Unallowed Repair [2 \text{ lakh} - 7\% \text{ of } 20\text{L}] | 0.6 lakh | — |
| **Closing WDV (for next year)** | **16.6 lakh** | **13.28 lakh** |

 **Allowable Expenses (Tax Deduction)**

 **i) Allowed Depreciation**
Since the car is used **50%** for business and **50%** for personal use, only half of the total depreciation is deductible.
 * **For 2079-80:** $50\% \times 4 \text{ lakh} = \mathbf{2 \text{ lakh}}$
 * **For 2080-81:** $50\% \times 3.32 \text{ lakh} = \mathbf{1.66 \text{ lakh}}$


**ii) Allowed Repair (For 2079-80)**
Repair is allowed at the **lower of**:
 1. Actual Repair: **Rs. 2 lakh**
 2. 7% of Depreciation Base: **Rs. 1.4 lakh** $(7\% \times 20\text{L})$
 * **Limit:** Rs. 1.4 lakh
 * **Business Portion (50%):** $50\% \times 1.4 \text{ lakh} = \mathbf{0.7 \text{ lakh}}$

**Notes / Explanation 
It means the allowance is only for the portion used for business; however, the **entire** depreciation value must be deducted from the block (asset pool) to determine the remaining value.

## Chapter 11


Questions

#### (IT) Question 1 chapter 11 (page: 11.7)

Question 1
Standard Chartered Bank has appointed AX Consultancy Pvt. Ltd. as Tax Consultant on Magh 20, 2070 and the effective date of contract is Shrawan 1, 2071. Under the contract the bank is liable to pay an Annual fee of Rs. 500,000. As per the contract, an advance amount of Rs. 100,000 is to be paid on appointment date (i.e. on Magh 20, 2070). AX Consultancy Pvt. Ltd. is in confusion whether the amount of Rs. 100,000 to be included in its income of Income Year 2070/071 as the appointment letter is already received during the Income Year.
Advise.

**Answer**

According to **Section 22 of Income Tax Act, 2058**, a company is required to maintain accounts on **accrual basis** for the purpose of tax.
Further, **Section 24** states that a person shall treat an amount as received and include it in income when it becomes **entitled to receive** the payment.
**In the given case:**
Ax Consultancy P. Ltd. is a company incorporated under prevailing company laws and is covered within the definition of company under **Income Tax Act, 2058**.
The company has made a contract on **Magh 20, 2070** (i.e., in 2069-70). However, the effective date of contract is **Shrawan 1, 2071** (i.e., 2070-71). It means the company is providing services from **2070-71** only.
**Thus:**
**Rs. 1,00,000** received on appointment date is an **advance payment**, which shall be accrued in **2070-71** only.
Since the company's right to receive the payment/income is established in **2070-71** only, the company can't include the amount of **Rs. 1,00,000** in its income in **2069-70**.



#### (IT) Question 2 chapter 11 (page: 11.7)

Question 2
Munal Trade Link is a various goods supplier. The trade link is using accrual basis of accounting for the income generated from the business. He gave an application to the department to change his basis of Accounting from accrual to cash in Income Year 2070/071. In accordance with the generally accepted accounting principles, Income Tax Department has given the approval to account for his income on cash basis in Income Year 2070/71. Some accounting information of the trade link at the end of 2069/070 before changing the basis of accounting, was as below:
i) Goods worth of Rs. 75,000 has already been supplied to a retailer in Income Year 2069/070 but amount has not been received. Advance Rs. 45,000 has been received from another retailer.
ii) Goods worth of Rs. 60,000 has been purchased from a vendor in Income Year 2069/070 but amount has not been paid. Advance Rs. 35,000 has been paid to another vendor.
iii) Communications and electricity expenses of Rs. 3,500 were paid on Bhadra, 2070. These expenses were incurred in Jestha and Ashadh, 2070.
State how you make adjustments of income and expense in FY 2070/071 in above conditions?

**Answer**

**As per section 22 of Income Tax Act, 2058; Murali Trade Link shall adjust the amounts as below:**

| S.N. | Particulars | Prior Treatment (Accrual) | Accounting under Cash Basis | Adjustment |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **i.** | Credit sales of 2069-70 | It's **included** in income as it was accrued in 69-70. | An amount is included in income when payment is received. | To avoid **duplication** of inclusion of the amount in income; **Rs. 75,000** shall not be included in income even if payment is received. |
| **ii.** | Advance received in 2069-70 | It **isn't included** in income as it wasn't accrued in 69-70. | An amount is included in income when payment is received. | To avoid **omission** of Rs. 45,000 from inclusion in both the years, it shall be **included** in income in 2070-71. |
| **iii.** | Credit purchase of 2069-70 | It's **deducted** from income as it was accrued in 2069-70. | An amount is deducted from income when payment is made. | To avoid **deduction** of expenses in both the years; **Rs. 60,000** shall not be deducted in 2070-71, even if payment is made. |
| **iv.** | Advance paid in 2069-70 | It's **not deducted** from income in 69-70 as it wasn't accrued in last year. | *(Same as iii above)* | To avoid **omission** of deduction of expenses in both the years; it shall be **deducted** from income of 2070-71. |
| **v.** | Exp. of 2069-70; paid in 2070-71. | It's **deducted** from income in 2069-70 as it was accrued in 2069-70. | *(Same as iii above)* | To avoid **duplication** of deduction of expenses; **Rs. 3,500** shall not be deducted in current year, even when payment is made. |

**Key Takeaways from Section 22:**
> When transitioning between accounting methods (Accrual to Cash or vice versa), adjustments must be made to ensure that:
>  1. No income or expense is counted **twice** (Duplication).
>  2. No income or expense is **missed** entirely (Omission).



#### (IT) Question 3 chapter 11 (page: 11.7)

Question 3
Mr. X is engaged in legal consultancy services. Mr. X has maintained his books of accounts on come year 2068/69. From income year 2069/70 he wants to changeover to accrual basis of accounting. Following information from records of his books of accounts of income year 2068/69 is abstracted.
i) Service provided but fee not received Rs. 5 Lakhs which was not included in the income.
ii) Advance received from customers but service not rendered Rs. 3 lakhs. The amount was included in the income in the income year 2068/69.
iii) Rent for the period 2069 Shrawan to 2069 Paush paid in Ashadh 2069 Rs 60,000. The amount is included in the expense in the income year 2068/69.
You are required to advise Mr. X.

**Answer**

| S.N.     | Particulars                                                           | Prior Treatment (Cash Basis)                              | Accounting under Accrual Basis                                           | Adjustment                                                                                                                                   |
| -------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **i.**   | **Credit sales of last year**<br> $\Rightarrow$ 5,00,000              | **Not included** in income as it wasn't received/accrued. | Included in income when **right to receive** the payment is established. | To avoid omission of inclusion of the amount in income in both the years; **Rs. 5 lakh shall be included** in income in current year.        |
| **ii.**  | **Advance received in last year**<br> $\Rightarrow$ 3 lakh            | **Included** in income as payment was received.           | Included in income when right to receive the payment is established.     | To avoid duplication of inclusion of the amount; **Rs. 3 lakh shall not be included** in income in current year even if service is provided. |
| **iii.** | **Expenses of 2069-70; paid in 2068-69**<br> $\Rightarrow$ Rs. 60,000 | **Deducted** from income as payment was made.             | Deducted from income **when it's accrued**.                              | To avoid duplication of deduction of exp.; it **shall not be deducted** in current year even if it's accrued in current year.                |

#### (IT) Question 5 chapter 11 (page: 11.8)

Question 5
Prabin & Chandra Co., a partnership concern was following cash basis of accounting until IY 2071/72. On 1st Kartik 2072, the concern filed an application to change the basis of accounting as it deemed the change will properly reflect the income of the firm on 15th Mangsir 2072 with effect from IY 2072/73. Your firm has been appointed as tax expert to scrutinize the effect of change in accounting on tax liability of the concern. Your firm decided to send you to identify the details, and you identified the following:
a. The partners of the concern disclosed that firm has provided service to a company during previous income years, but the amount is still receivable as on the date of your scrutiny. Such outstanding service fee in relation to service provided but fee not received is Rs. 10 Lakhs. The amount is expected to be received in early 2073/74.
b. The concern is operating at a premise. The owner of the premise is at America since eight years. The owner has no bank account in Nepal and there is no other means to send the rental expenses to the owner. As such, Rs. 400,000 related to rental expenses of last four years is still not paid and expected to be paid during IY 2074/75, when the owner of the premise returns to Nepal.
c. The concern is implementing a multi-year service contract beginning from IY 1st Kartik 2070 and ending on 31st Chaitra 2075. The total service fee of Rs. 6,600,000 has been received in advance on 1st Jestha 2072, and included in income during the IY 2071/72.
Prepare a memorandum to your partner indicating the correct treatment of the above matters as per Income Tax Act.

**Answer**

**Accounting Adjustments: Change from Cash to Accrual Basis**

| S.N.   | Particulars                                                                          | Prior Treatment (Cash)                                        | Accounting under Accrual Basis                        | Treatment (Adjustment)                                                                                                                                              |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **a.** | **Service Provided but fee not received** $\Rightarrow$ 10L                          | Not included in income as payment wasn't received.            | Included in income when payment is **accrued**.       | Rs. 10 lakh shall be included in income to avoid omission from inclusion in both the years.                                                                         |
| **b.** | **Expenses of previous years haven't been paid till date** $\Rightarrow$ Rs. 400,000 | Not deducted from income as payment wasn't made.              | Deducted from income when the payment is **accrued**. | To avoid omission of deduction of expenses; Rs. 400,000 shall be deducted from income of current year.                                                              |
| **c.** | **Service fee received in advance**                                                  | It's included in income as payment has already been received. | Included in income when payment is **accrued**.       | To avoid duplication of inclusion of the amount Rs. 45,00,000 shall be reversed from income and it shall be included in income as and when it's accrued. **(WN-1)** |

**Working Note - 1**
**Calculation of Income to be Reversed**
 * **Total contract period:** 1st Kartik, 2070 — 31st Chaitra, 2075 $\Rightarrow$ **66 months**
 * **Total contract amount:** **Rs. 66 lakhs**
 * **Contract period before change of accounting method:** 1st Kartik, 2070 — 31st Ashadh, 2072 $\Rightarrow$ **21 months**
 **Step 1: Amount accrued before change of method**

$\text{Amount Accrued} = \frac{66 \text{ lakh}}{66 \text{ month}} \times 21 \text{ months}$

$\Rightarrow \text{Rs. 21 lakh}$

 **Step 2: Amount to be reversed**
 
| Description | Amount (Lakhs) |
|---|---|
| Total amount included in income (Cash Basis) | 66 lakh |
| Less: Amount actually accrued | (21 lakh) |
| **Amount / Income to be reversed** | **45 lakh** |



#### (IT) Question 6 chapter 11 (page: 11.8)

Question 6
Oppo International Pvt. Ltd., Kathmandu is a dealer of Oppo Electronics. The Oppo Electronics are manufactured in China by Oppo International. The company imported the electronics goods worth of US $ 50,000 on 23 Baishakh, 2074, the payment was done on 23 Shrawan, 2074. The exchange rate of the transaction was as follows:
 * Baishakh 23: Rs. 104 per US * Closing of Ashadh: Rs. 107 per US
 * Shrawan 23: Rs. 106 per US $
   You are required to compute the amount for inclusions or deductions for the tax assessment of this transaction mentioning the relevant provisions of Income Tax Act, 2058. \[5 Marks-CAP II-Dec 2017]

**Answer**

As per **Sec. 22 of Income Tax Act, 2058**, the company shall account for their income by following the **accrual basis of accounting**.
Further, **Sec. 24** states that under the accrual basis, expenses are deducted from income when the obligation to make the payment is created.
And **Sec. 24(4)** states that when there's a change in the amount received / expense incurred due to fluctuation in foreign exchange rates, it shall be adjusted while receiving or making the payment.

In the given case, **Oppo International P. Ltd.** is a company. Thus, it accounts for its income under the accrual basis of accounting.
Thus, the company has to record expenses as per the exchange rate prevailing as on **Baisakh 23**.
 $\therefore$ **Purchase** $\Rightarrow 104 \times 50,000$
 $\Rightarrow \text{Rs. } 52,00,000$

Assuming there's no opening and closing stock, **Rs. 52 lakh** shall be allowed as a deduction u/s 15 in **2073-74**.
However, the company is making payment on **Shrawan 23, 2074**.
 
 Thus, **Total Payment** $\Rightarrow 106 \times 50,000$
 $\Rightarrow \text{Rs. } 53,00,000$
 
The difference of **Rs. 1 lakh** is due to fluctuation in exchange rates and it's allowed as a deduction in **2074-75** (i.e., at the time of payment).

**Note:** Fluctuation in exchange rate as on Ashadh end is irrelevant as the difference shall be adjusted only at the time of receiving or making the payment.




#### (IT) Question 7 chapter 11 (page: 11.8)

Question 7
City Computers Pvt. Ltd. is dealer of Acer Laptops. It imports the laptops from abroad and sells it to the local computer shops all over Nepal. The company had imported laptops worth USD 1,00,000 which reached in Birgunj Custom Office on Magh 25, 2078, however the company had received invoice issued by foreign company on Magh 1, 2078. Due to disturbance in transportation, the company had to pay 50,000 as demurrage charge and custom duty 12,50,000. The company made payment to the foreign company on Chaitra 20, 2078. Following are the exchange rates:
 * Magh 1, 2078: 1USD = Rs. 120
 * Magh 25, 2078: 1USD = Rs. 123
 * Chaitra 20, 2078: 1USD = Rs. 118
   Calculate the cost of purchase to be considered under section 15 and gain/loss in foreign exchange transaction citing relevant provisions of Income Tax Act, 2058. \[5 Marks-CAP II-June 2022]

**Answer**

As per **Sec. 22 of Income Tax Act, 2058**, the company shall account for their income by following the **accrual basis of accounting**.
Further, **Sec. 24** states that under the accrual basis, expenses are deducted from income when the obligation to make the payment is created.
And **Sec. 24(4)** states that when there's a change in the amount received / expense incurred due to fluctuation in foreign exchange rates, it shall be adjusted while receiving or making the payment.

In the given case, the company has received the invoice on **Magh 1, 2078**. Thus, the company shall account for the expenses as of that date under the **accrual basis of accounting**.
Thus, the **allowable cost of purchase** is computed as:

| Particulars | Amount (Lakhs) |
|---|---|
| Purchase cost (120 \times 1 \text{ lakh}) | 120 lakh |
| (+) Custom duty | 12.5 lakh |
| (+) Demurrage charge | 0.5 lakh |
| **Total** | **133 lakh** |

Assuming there is no opening and closing stock, it is allowed as a deduction u/s 15.
Further, the company is making payment to its creditor on **Chaitra 20, 2078**.
 
**Total payment to be made** $\Rightarrow 118 \times 1 \text{ lakh}$
$\Rightarrow \text{Rs. } 118 \text{ lakh}$

The difference of **Rs. 2 lakh** is due to fluctuation in exchange rates. Thus, it shall be included in income u/s 7(2) at the time of making the payment (i.e., on **Chaitra 20, 2078**).
   



#### (IT) Question 8 chapter 11 (page: 11.8)

Question 8
Following are details regarding Nepal Construction Company Limited which has obtained construction contract from Department of Road for Rs. 110,000,000 for period of four years.
Compute taxable income of company for the financial year 2070-71.

| Particulars | Amount |
|---|---|
| Total estimated expenses | 100,000,000 |
| Total expenses during F.Y. 2070-71 | 22,000,000 |
Would your answer be different if % completion of work, as certified by engineers was 25%?


**Answer**

Computation of taxable income 

| S.N.  | Particulars                               | Calculation                  | Amount          |
| ----- | ----------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | --------------- |
| **A** | Total Contract Revenue                    | -                            | 110 Million     |
| **B** | Total Estimated Exp.                      | -                            | 100 Million     |
| **C** | Cost incurred till date                   | -                            | 22 Million      |
| **D** | % completion                              | $[\frac{C}{B} \times 100\%]$ | 22%             |
| **E** | Amount to be included in income till date | $[A \times D]$               | 24.2 Million    |
| **F** | Income till date                          | [E - C]                      | 2.2 Million     |
| **G** | Income till last year                     | -                            | —               |
|       | **Taxable Income**                        | [F - G]                      | **2.2 Million** |

**If % completion as certified by engineer was 25%:**
 * **i) Amt. to be included in income:** 27.5 Million
   * *Calculation:* 110 \text{ Million} \times 25\%
 * **ii) Cost incurred till date:** 22 Million
 * **iii) Income till date / Taxable income:** 5.5 Million
   * *Calculation:* 27.5 \text{ Million} - 22 \text{ Million}

ABC ltd has entered into a construction contract with PQR Ltd with a term of 3 years. The contract price and the total estimated cost are Rs 60 Billion and 40 Billion respectively. In Year 1, cost amounting to Rs 10 Billion was incurred and up to Year 2 Rs 25 Billion has been expended.
Calculate the income of ABC ltd to be included in the concerned head of income with respect to long term contract in Year 1, Year 2 and Year 3. Briefly explain long term contracts, excluded contracts and deferred return.

**Answer**

**(Rs. in billion)**

| Particulars | Year - 1 | Year - 2 | Year - 3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A) Contract Revenue** | 60 | 60 | 60 |
| **B) Estimated Cost** | 40 | 40 | 40 |
| **C) Cost incurred till date** | 10 | 25 | 40 |
| **D) % completion till date** (\frac{C}{B} \times 100\%) | 25% | 62.5% | 100% |
| **E) Amt. to be included in income till date** (A \times D) | 15 | 37.5 | 60 |
| **F) Income till date** (E - C) | 5 | 12.5 | 20 |
| **G) Income till last yr** | — | 5 | 12.5 |
| **Taxable Income** (F - G) | **5** | **7.5** | **7.5** |

#### (IT) Question 5 chapter 12 (page: 12.11)

5. Mr. A and Mr. B contributed Rs. 4 million and Rs. 6 million respectively and deposited into a common bank account...
to be used to purchase a plot of land and to resale the same in the future. After 6 months of depositing the money into the bank account, they purchased a plot of land for Rs. 9 million and got the land registered in their joint name. Expenses on registration and commission at the time of purchase amounted to Rs. 1 million. Later, they sold the plot of land for Rs. 17.2 million. Also, they paid Rs. 1.2 million as sales commission and other incidental expenses pertinent to the disposal. The bank has given interest @ 12% per annum on such deposit.
Show tax implication of gain on disposal of land and interest income.

**Answer**

**Provision:** (As mentioned above)

In the given case, Mr. A & Mr. B have deposited the amount into a common bank account. Further, they have also purchased a land in their joint name. These are investments under joint ownership as per **sec. 30**.

Thus, gain/income in such case shall be treated as below:

**Sale of Land**

It is a disposal of **Non-Business chargeable assets**. Thus, its gain is treated as below:

|**Particulars**|**Amount (Million)**|**Amount (Million)**|
|---|---|---|
|**Incoming u/s 39**||**17.2**|
|**(-) Outgoing u/s 38**|||
|i) Purchase cost|9||
|ii) Registration exp|1||
|iii) Sales commission|1.2|**(11.2)**|
|**Total Gain u/s 37**||**6 Million**|

**Allocation of Gain:**

- $\therefore \text{Mr. A's share} = 6 \text{ Million} \times \frac{4}{10} \implies \text{Rs. 2.4 Million}$
    
- $\therefore \text{Mr. B's share} \implies \text{Rs. 3.6 Million}$
    

**Note:** These amounts shall be included in their respective income.

**Interest Income**

|**Particulars**|**Amount**|
|---|---|
|Total interest ($10 \text{ Million} \times 12\% \times \frac{6}{12}$)|600,000|
|(-) TDS @ 6% (final)|(36,000)|
|**Net Interest**|**564,000**|

**Allocation of Interest:**

- $\therefore \text{Mr. A's share} = 564,000 \times \frac{4}{10} = 225,600$
    
- $\therefore \text{Mr. B's share} = 564,000 - 225,600 \implies \text{Rs. 338,400}$
    

**Note:** However, these amounts shall **not** be included in their respective income (as they are subject to final withholding tax).

to be used to purchase a plot of land to resale the same later on. #### (IT) Question 7 chapter 12 (page: 12.11)

7. AB (Pvt.) Ltd has made an agreement with ABC (Pvt.) Ltd to arrange the cash flow for its transactions.
Chairman of AB (Pvt.) Ltd is also a representative board member of ABC (Pvt.) Ltd. In Shrawan 2072, loan amounting Rs. 50 million had been provided by the ABC to the AB. Interest rate was 10%. Both companies are located at Kathmandu. Tax Officer denied the interest expenses of Rs. 5 million claimed by AB (Pvt.) Ltd. on the ground that the transaction is a related party's transaction.
Answer whether the Tax Officer's assessment is correct?

**Answer**

According to the provision of **Section 33 of Income Tax Act, 2058**, the IRD may issue a written notice distributing, allocating, or appropriating the amount of inclusion or deduction in case of transactions between associated persons that aren't carried out at **arm's length price**.

Generally, transactions between associates are deemed to be carried out at arm's length price.

In the given case, a loan of **Rs. 50 Million** has been provided by **ABC P. Ltd.** to **AB Pvt. Ltd.** in which the latter has provided interest to the former. The loan has been provided @ **interest rate of 10%**.

It means the transaction seems to be carried out at arm's length price only.

Further, deduction of interest expenses is allowable **u/s 14(1)** if the provisions of **Section 14(2)** aren't applicable.

In the given case, none of the conditions have been satisfied for the applicability of Section 14(2). Thus, interest expenses are fully allowable **u/s 14(1)**.

The contention of the tax officer to disallow interest expenses simply on the ground of being a related party transaction is **wrong**.

Computation of Taxable Income & Tax Liability
**Name:** Mr. Narayan

A) Inclusion u/s 9 (2)

| Particulars | Amount (NPR) |
| :--- | :---: |
| Natural resources payment [170,000 / 85%] | 200,000 |
| Interest income from ABC Ltd. [21,25,000 / 85%] | 2,500,000 |
| Compensation | 25,000 |
| Gift | 50,000 |
| **Total Inclusions** | **2,775,000** |

B) Deductions

| Particulars | Amount (NPR) |
| :--- | :---: |
| i) Depreciation u/s 19 | 5,500 |
| ii) General deduction u/s 13 (Exp. relating to natural resources) | 8,500 |
| **Total Deductions** | **14,000** |

C) Assessable Income from Investment / Total Assessable Income \[A - B]
**Total:** **2,761,000**

D) Reduction
**i) Donation to tax exempt entity u/s 12** (Lower of):
- 5% of Adjusted Taxable Income (5% × 2,761,000) = 138,050
- Actual = 40,000
- Limit = 100,000
- **Selected Deduction:** **(40,000)**

E) Taxable Income \[C - D]
**Total:** **2,721,000**

F) Deductions / Zero Rated Income
**Life / Investment Insurance Premium** (Lower of):
- Actual = 22,500
- Limit = 40,000

G) Tax Liability

| :--- | :---: | :---: |

Net Tax Payable

#### (IT) Question 2 chapter 13 (page: 13.2)

2. Tax Liability Calculation (Mr. A)
From the following information, calculate tax liability of Mr. A, a Nepali citizen who is a resident natural person for I.Y. 2072/73:

| Particulars | Amount (Rs.) |
|---|---|
| Income earned from lending to a private company of Nepal (net of TDS) | 1,275,000 |
| House Rent Income earned | 900,000 |
| Dividend from Australian Company not having effective management in Nepal | 500,000 |
| Dividend from a partnership firm in Nepal | 300,000 |
| Royalty from a book publisher in Nepal (gross) | 250,000 |
| Income from right to use his land in Nepal by a water supplier against extraction of water (net of TDS) | 170,000 |
| House rent income from leasing of his property at Australia | 100,000 |
| Gain from a resident Investment Insurance company (net of TDS) | 190,000 |
| Market Value of Gift received by him at the time of attending AGM of a resident company | 80,000 |
| Gain on Disposal of land situated in Nepal (Disposal proceed of Rs. 5 Million) | 500,000 |
Note: Will your answer be different if he was a resident person?

**Answer**

Computation of Taxable Income & Tax Liability**
**For Income Year 2072-73**

**A. Inclusion u/s 9(2)**

|**Particulars**|**Calculation**|**Amount (Rs.)**|
|---|---|---|
|Interest from Pvt. Company|$\frac{1,275,000}{85\%}$|1,500,000|
|Dividend from Australian Co.||500,000|
|Royalty||250,000|
|Natural Resources Payment|$\frac{170,000}{85\%}$|200,000|
|Gift||80,000|
|Gain on disposal of NBCA||500,000|
|**Total Assessable Income**||**3,030,000**|

**B. Tax Liability Calculation**

**i) On Income (Other than gain on disposal of NBCA): Rs. 2,530,000**

- First Rs. 5,00,000 @ 0% = **Nil**
    
- Next Rs. 2,00,000 @ 10% = **20,000**
    
- Next Rs. 3,00,000 @ 20% = **60,000**
    
- Next Rs. 10,00,000 @ 30% = **300,000**
    
- Balance Rs. 5,30,000 @ 36% = **190,800**
    
- **Sub-Total (i): 570,800**
    

**ii) On Gain on Disposal of NBCA**

- Rs. 500,000 @ 5% (Assumed ownership > 5 yrs) = **25,000**
    

**Total Tax Liability (i + ii): Rs. 595,800**

**C. Net Tax Payable**

|**Particulars**|**Calculation**|**Amount (Rs.)**|
|---|---|---|
|**Total Tax Liability**||**595,800**|
|_Less:_ Advance Tax u/s 95A(5)|$500,000 \times 5\%$|(25,000)|
|_Less:_ TDS Receivable|$[\frac{170,000}{85\%} \times 15\%] + [\frac{1,275,000}{85\%} \times 15\%]$|(255,000)|
|**Net Tax Payable**||**315,800**|



**3. Notes and Special Considerations**

- **Non-Resident Status:** If Mr. A were a non-resident, all income would be subject to **Final Withholding**, except for the gain on disposal of NBCA, which would attract tax at **25%**.
    
- **Exclusions:**
    
    - Bank interest, dividends from resident companies (e.g., Sanima Bank), and investment insurance gains are often subject to final withholding and thus excluded from total assessable income.
        
    - House rent income earned by a natural person is generally not included in the tax base for central income tax (often handled by local government/municipalities).

Mrs. Kriti is an employee in Nepal Bank Ltd. Besides employment income, she has the following transactions for Income Year 20X-73/X-74. Sort out the income from investment and tax liability for the year assuming that 0% and 10% tax slabs are already over by income from employment.

| S.N. | Particulars | Amount (Rs.) |
|---|---|---|
| 1. | Net gain on disposal of listed shares | 100,000.00 |
| 2. | Purchase of New listed shares | 600,000.00 |
| 3. | Income from Taxi | 400,000.00 |
| 4. | Gain on sale of gold | 100,000.00 |
| 5. | Interest from savings deposit from 'A' class bank | 4,000.00 |
| 6. | Interest from fixed deposit from 'A' class bank | 40,000.00 |
| 7. | Net gain from disposal of ancestral house (owned and resided since Bikram Sambat 20X-60) | 5,000,000.00 |
| 8. | Royalty from music video | 100,000.00 |
| 9. | House rent | 300,000.00 |
| 10. | Shares transaction commission expenses | 5,000.00 |
| 11. | Salary to taxi driver and other taxi related expenses | 200,000.00 |
| 12. | Music video expenses | 20,000.00 |
| 13. | House repair and maintenance expenses | 20,000.00 |
\[CAP II - June - 2019 - 10 Marks]

**Answer**

**Computation of Taxable Income & Tax Liability**

**Name of Assessee:** Mrs. Kriti
**Income Year:** 20x3-x4

**A) Inclusion u/s 9(2)**

|**Particulars**|**Amount (Rs.)**|
|---|---|
|Net gain on disposal of shares|100,000|
|Royalty from music video|100,000|
|**Total (A)**|**200,000**|



**B) Deduction**

|**Particulars**|**Amount (Rs.)**|
|---|---|
|i) General ded$^n$ u/s 13 (Music video expenses)|20,000|



**Income Summary**

|**Section**|**Particulars**|**Amount (Rs.)**|
|---|---|---|
|**C)**|Income from Investment [A - B]|180,000|
|**D)**|Income from employment|700,000|
|**E)**|**Total Assessable Income (C + D)**|**880,000**|



**F) Tax Liability**

**i) On income other than gain on disposal of NBCA (Rs. 780,000):**

- First Rs. 5 Lakh @ 1%: **Rs. 5,000**
    
- Next Rs. 2 Lakh @ 10%: **Rs. 20,000**
    
- Balance Rs. 80,000 @ 20%: **Rs. 16,000**
    
- **Sub-total (i): Rs. 41,000**
    

**ii) On gain on disposal of NBCA:**

- Rs. 100,000 x 5% (Assumed ownership > 365 days): **Rs. 5,000**
    

**Total Tax Liability (i + ii): Rs. 46,000**



**G) Net Tax Payable**

|**Particulars**|**Calculation**|**Amount (Rs.)**|
|---|---|---|
|**Total Tax Liability**||**46,000**|
|_Less:_ Advance collection of tax u/s 95A(2)|(100,000 x 5%)|(5,000)|
|**Net Tax Payable**||**41,000**|



**Explanatory Notes**

1. **Hiring of Vehicles:** Income earned by a natural person from hiring vehicles is taxed per Sec. 1(13) of Sch. 1 and is not included in this computation.
    
2. **Gold:** Assumed to be held for personal purposes; thus, it is a private asset and not included in income computation.
    
3. **Private Building:** If owned for $\geq 10$ years and resided in for $\geq 10$ years, it is not classified as a Non-Business Chargeable Asset (NBCA), and income is excluded.
    
4. **Interest Income:** Interest received by an individual from a resident bank or financial institution attracts a 5% (final) withholding tax and is not included in total income.
    
5. **House Rent:** Rental income earned by a natural person is not classified as taxable income for this specific individual assessment (often handled locally).

|**Net Tax Payable [H - I - J]**||**52,900**|

- **Exemption u/s 47A:** Dividend distributed by a merged/amalgamated bank, financial institution (FI), or insurance company, and income earned by a shareholder from the disposal of shares within 2 years of merger/amalgamation, is exempt from tax.







#### (IT) Question 6 chapter 13 (page: 13.4)

6. Assessment for Mr. Oli (Disabled Person, Manang District)
Mr. Oli, a disable person and is residing in Manang District, disclosed his following income & expenses for the income year 20X-71/X-72.
Income side:
 * a. Interest on private money lending activities: Rs. 127,500 (after TDS)
 * b. Royalty from natural resources: Rs. 306,000 (after TDS)
 * c. Interest from bank deposit: Rs. 190,000
 * d. Dividend from domestic companies: Rs. 95,000
 * e. Royalty from writing article: Rs. 15,000
 * f. Royalty from book publication: Rs. 170,000 (after TDS)
 * g. Compensation received: Rs. 50,000
 * h. Money found lying in street: Rs. 7,500 (net)
Expense side:
 * a. Interest on private money lending: Rs. 2,000
 * b. Natural resources: Rs. 1,000
 * c. Royalty from writing article: Rs. 100
 * d. Dividend from domestic companies: Rs. 500
 * e. Legal expenses (income tax appeal): Rs. 5,000
 * f. Salary to assistant: Rs. 30,000
 * g. Life insurance premium: Rs. 15,000 on his own policy amount Rs. 300,000
 * h. Contribution to approved retirement fund: Rs. 50,000
 * i. Donation to tax exempt organization: Rs. 12,000
Required: Compute the tax liability and net tax payable of Mr. Oli for the income year 20X-71/X-72. (Assume him as couple for calculation).
\[CAP II-June-2016-10 Marks]

**Answer**

**Computation of Taxable Income & Tax Liability**

**A) Inclusion u/s 9(2)**

|**Particulars**|**Calculation**|**Amount (Rs.)**|
|---|---|---|
|Interest on private money lending|$\frac{127,500}{85\%}$|150,000|
|Royalty from natural resources|$\frac{306,000}{85\%}$|360,000|
|Royalty from articles writing||15,000|
|Royalty from book|$\frac{170,000}{85\%}$|200,000|
|Compensation||50,000|
|**Total Inclusion (A)**||**775,000**|



**B) Deductions**

|**Particulars**|**Calculation**|**Amount (Rs.)**|
|---|---|---|
|i) Interest exp. u/s 14||2,000|
|ii) General ded$^n$ u/s 13:|||
|* Natural resources|1,000||
|* Royalty|100||
|* Legal exp.|5,000||
|* Salary|30,000|36,100|
|**Total Deductions (B)**||**38,100**|



**C) Assessable Income [A - B]**

**Amount: Rs. 736,900**



**D) Reductions**

**i) Contribution to ARF u/s 63 (Lower of):**

- $\frac{1}{3}$ of assessable income: **Rs. 245,633**
    
- Actual: **Rs. 50,000**
    
- Limit: **Rs. 300,000**
    
- **Eligible Reduction:** **(50,000)**
    

**iv) Donation to tax exempt entity u/s 12 (Lower of):**

- 5% of ATI i.e. $5\% \times (736,900 - 50,000) = 34,345$
    
- Actual: **Rs. 12,000**
    
- Limit: **Rs. 100,000**
    
- **Eligible Reduction:** **(12,000)**
    



**Taxable Income Summary**

|**Description**|**Amount (Rs.)**|
|---|---|
|**Taxable Income**|**674,900**|
|_Less:_ Remote Area allowances|(50,000)|
|_Less:_ Disabled allowances ($600,000 \times 50\%$)|(300,000)|
|_Less:_ Life insurance prem$^m$ (Lower of Actual: 15,000 or Limit: 40,000)|(15,000)|
|**Balance Taxable Income**|**309,900**|



**Tax Liability & Net Payable**

|**Particulars**|**Calculation**|**Amount (Rs.)**|
|---|---|---|
|**Tax Liability**||**-**|
|_Less:_ TDS Receivable|$[150,000 \times 15\% + 360,000 \times 15\% + 200,000 \times 15\%]$|**(106,500)**|
|**Net tax Payable/C/f to next year**||**(106,500)**|


**Note**

1. Money found lying in the street should be reported to the concerned authority or police rather than being included in income.

#### (IT) Question 1 chapter 14 (page: 14.7)

 1. Khaire Private Limited is a US based company whose majority of board meetings were held in Nepal. It is making interest payment of Rs. 20 lakh to a Japanese bank. Is there any tax withholding implications?

**Answer**
Since, majority of board meeting were held in Nepal the company is resident in Nepal 
Thus the interest paid by the company is said to have source in Nepal. 
Hence the company is required to deduct tax at source@15%

#### (IT) Question 16 chapter 14 (page: 14.8)

 16. Nepal Infrastructure company Limited a company registered in Nepal holds 70% of Japan Infrastructure Company Limited, a company registered in Japan. During an income year 2072-73 JICL distributed dividend amounting Rs. 35,00,000 and dividend tax 10%. During an income year 2073-74 company earned profit of Rs. 1,00,00,000 and paid Rs. 29,00,000 as corporate tax. NICL received Rs. 35,00,000 as dividend during income year 2073-74. Explain tax treatment.

**Answer**
According to section 69 of income tax act 2058 resident person shall include proportionate amount of associated income of control foreign entity
Further, no other amount (except proportionated amount of associated income) eg: dividend shall be included in income of a resident person in respect of income earned by controlled foreign entity
Also the resident person may claim foreign tax credit u/s section 71 for the tax paid corporate tax or dividend tax by control foreign entity
In the given case NICL holds seven....or an entity under section sixty nine
In the given case NICL holds seventy percent shares in JICL thus JICL is a conclude for an entity under section 69
Thus, tax implication for various transaction shall be as below:

For IY 2072-73
a. Dividend distributed by JICL shall not be included in income of NICL
b. NICL may claim foreign tax credit u/s 71 of tax paid in foreign country i.e. (35,00,000 x 10% = 2,45,000)

For IY 2073-74
a. Amount to be included in income of NICL
→ 1,00,00,000 x 70% = Rs 70L
b. NICL may claim foreign tax credit u/s 71 of corporate tax paid by JICL 
→ 29,00,000 x 70%  = 20,30,000
c. Dividend shall not be included in Income of NICL. 
d. NICL may claim foreign tax credit if dividend tax is paid in foreign country by JICL




#### (IT) Question 17 chapter 14 (page: 14.8)

 17. Nepal Construction Company Limited, a company registered in Nepal holds 60% share of Bhutan Construction Company Limited, a company registered in Bhutan. Bhutan Construction Company Limited received contract to construct commercial building at Kathmandu. It ran for 9 months & earned Rs. 100 lacs as business income. Explain tax treatment in hands of Nepal construction Co. Ltd.

**Answer**
NCC(Nepal) → 60% →BCC(Bhutan) → PE(Nepal)

~~Taxable income 100L
<u>income tax 25L</u>
net income repatriated 75L
Repatriation tax(5%) 3.75L
Now, it shall be included in income of BCCL. Thus, it becomes part of associated income and again shall be included in NCCL's income~~

>It creates double taxation and foreign tax credit u/s 71 cannot be availed , because source of income is in Nepal

So, the income earned by foreign PE of non-resident shall be allocated to it's owner with respect to section 69

<u>with respect to NCCL</u>
100L x 60% = 60L 
shall be included in NCCL's income

<u>with respect to other owners</u>
Income = 40L
<u>Tax @ 25% = 10L</u>
Income repatriated = 30L
repatriation tax = 1.5L
This, 30L will not be included in income of NCCL

### Question 11 (page: 35)

It receives deposit from dealers for such glass bottle supplied to them at the time of sale of beverages. As per the contract between Company and its dealers, the deposit shall be refunded to the dealers at the time of full and final settlement i.e. closer of business of dealers. It is also,agreed that the shortage of bottle shall be adjusted at the prevailing cost price to the company at full & final settlement date. A tax officer during his inspection of the company, found that the bottle worth Rs.30 Lacs ( 150,000 bottles at average rate of Rs. 20) were not in physical stock. He makes tax assessment order and treated the shortage of bottles as sold by the company. The company has proved that the bottles were sent to dealers; deposit has been received and shown as liability under "Bottles Deposits" in accounts. However the tax officer did not accept the contention and charged tax & penalty on such shortage. Meanwhile a dealer came with application to close the business and returned only 40,000 bottles which is lower than supplied quantity i.e.50,000 bottles and his deposit was Rs. 12.50 Lacs at the rate of Rs.25 each bottle but prevailing cost to the company as on date of closure of business is Rs. 36 per bottle.

Required:
i. Is contention of Tax officer correct?
i. What is to be done while doing full & final settlement of dealer as per VAT Act?

**Answer:**
As per sec 12(8) Value Added Tax Act, 2052, the deposits given in respect of goods/services shall not be held to taxable value unless supplier applies the deposit as consideration of supply.
In the given case, tasty cola Pvt Ltd is a company manufacturing beverages who sells its product on reusable glass receiving deposits for such glass supplied to the customer
since the amount is received on account  of deposits and ownership of the bottle is not transferred , it cannot be considered as sale of bottles unless Tasty cola pvt. ltd. applies it for consideration of supply i.e. unless there occurs shortage of bottles etc.
Thus, applying above provision in the given case, it can be concluded that contention of the taxpayer to treat shortage of bottles as sales of the company is incorrect. It was similarly held in case of Pepsi cola Nepal pvt. ltd. v/s IRD



b. Treatment at the time of full and final settlement
Total quantity of bottles supplied 50,000
Quantity of bottle returned 40,000
shortage 10,000
 
Deposit amount  Rs. 12,50,000
less: shortage (10,000 x 36)  Rs. 3,60,000
les: VAT on shortage (3,60,000 x 13%) Rs. 46,800
Net amount to be refunded on full and final settlement Rs. 8,43,200

> [!note] 
>  deduct cost and vat of 10,000 units of shortage and return  the remaining amount of deposit
> Pepsi cola Nepal pvt. ltd. shall deposit Rs 46,800 with IRD as it is VAT collected on sales

### Question 14 (page: 37)

Lumbini District Development Committee has called up an auction to sell its fixed assets (Computer, Furniture & Fixtures) as it is shifting to new office with modern infrastructures. Detail of amount collected from auction is as follows;
 * Computer: 50,000
 * Table: 30,000
 * Chairs: 40,000
 * Furniture: 1,40,000
 * Miscellaneous Assets: 70,000
 * Total amount received: 3,30,000

The officer of Lumbini District Development Committee did not collect VAT on this sale and did not issue VAT invoice as the sale price is less than the cost? Is he correct? Answer in the light of VAT Act, 2052. Will your answer be different if these goods were sold by a registered person dealing in the second hand goods?


**Answer**
As per provision of section 17(5) of VAT Act, 2052 and rule 33 of VAT rule 2053 , in case of used goods sold by a person dealing in used goods, VAT shall be collected on balance between sales price and cost price(including tax)
In the given case, Lumbini District Development Committee has sold used goods through auction. Since, it is not a person dealing with used goods, section 17(5) is not applicable
However, as per provision of section 15(3) GON, provincial government, public institutions etc. are required to collect VAT on sale of taxable transaction even if they are not registered in VAT. 
Thus, it shall collect VAT of 
=> 3,30,000 x 13% = Rs 42,900
 
However , if these goods are sold by registered person dealing in used goods , the provision of section 17(5) is applicable . Since, the sales price is less than cost price, no VAT shall levied.

| Details                                            | Amount in Rs |
| -------------------------------------------------- | ------------ |

| Purchase of raw material - Import                  | 5,500,000    |
| Purchase of packing material                       | 500,000      |
| Purchase of motorcycle                             | 150,000      |
| Purchase of bus for staff                          | 1,000,000    |
| Purchase of delivery van                           | 800,000      |
| Purchase of diesel for staff bus                   | 50,000       |
| Purchase of petrol for van/ motorbike              | 25,000       |
| Payment of consultancy service to a party in India | 90,000       |
| Telephone expenses                                 | 15,000       |
| Dealers meet expenses                              | 45,000       |
| Miscellaneous expenses                             | 85,000       |
| Opening VAT receivable                             | 9,000        |
All above items are excluding VAT
Miscellaneous expenses includes coke and Fanta purchased for dealers meet for Rs 10,000 and gift items purchased though abbreviated tax invoice of Rs. 25,000



<u>Answer</u>

Computation of VAT payable/receivable of M/S makalu food and beverages 
for the month of Poush 2069

|       | Particulars                                                | Amount       |
| ----- | ---------------------------------------------------------- | ------------ |
| A     | <u>VAT collected on sales</u>                              |              |
|       | - Local sales                                              |              |
|       | - Export sales                                             |              |
| B     | <u>Input tax credit</u>                                    |              |
|       | Raw material purchase<br>(45,00,000 x 13%)                 | 5,85,000     |
|       | Raw material import<br>(55,00,000 x 13%)                   | 7,15,000     |
|       | Packing material <br>(5,00,000 x 13%)                      | 65,000       |
|       | Motorcycle <br>(1,50,000 x 13%)                            | 19,500       |
|       | Purchase of bus for staff<br>(10,00,000 x 13% x 40%)       | 52,000       |
|       | Purchase of delivery van<br>(8,00,000 x 13%)               | 1,04,000     |
|       | Diesel for staff bus<br>(50,000 x 13%)                     | 6,500        |
|       | Petrol for van                                             | -            |
|       | Payment for consultancy service<br>(90,000 x 13%)          | 11,700       |
|       | Telephone expense<br>(15,000 x 13%)                        | 1,950        |
|       | Dealer's meet expense                                      | -            |
|       | Miscellaneous expenses<br>(85,000 - 10,000 - 25,000) x 13% | <u>6,500</u> |
|       | Net VAT payable/(receivable) for the month                 | (15,67,150)  |
| less: | opening VAT credit                                         | <u>9,000</u> |
|       | Net vat payable/(receivable)                               | (15,76,150)  |
notes:
1. In case of purchase of 3 or more wheeled passenger carrying vehicles , only 40% of VAT paid is allowed as credit
   Thus, 40% VAT is allowed in case of bus van , being goods carrying vehicle , 100% of VAT is allowed as credit
2. It is assumed that reverse VAT applicable in case of import of consultancy services from India is already paid
3. Dealer's meet expenses is assumed as entertainment expenses and hence, no deduction/ credit is allowed
4. VAT paid on beverages or that of abbreviated tax invoice is not allowed as credit

### Example question section-24(4)

<u>Question</u>


| (in lakhs)   | Baisakh | Jestha | Ashadh |
| ------------ | ------- | ------ | ------ |
| sales        | 20      | 5      | 30     |
| Export sales | 2       | 50     | 15     |
| Purchase     | 30      | 60     | 50     |
calculate VAT refund/credit that can be claimed

<u>Answer</u>

|     | Particulars                                      | Baisakh            | jestha          | Ashadh              |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------ | ------------------ | --------------- | ------------------- |
| A   | VAT collected on sales                           |                    |                 |                     |
|     | - Local sales @ 13%                              | 2.6                | 0.65            | 3.9                 |
|     | - Export sales @ 0%                              | <u>   -   </u>     | <u>   -   </u>  | <u>   -   </u>      |
|     |                                                  | 2.6                | 0.65            | 3.9                 |
| B   | Input tax credit                                 |                    |                 |                     |
|     | - Purchase @ 13%                                 | 3.9                | 7.8             | 6.5                 |
| C   | Net VAT payable/(receivable) for the month (A-B) | (1.3)              | (7.5)           | (2.6)               |
| D   | Refund claim                                     | <u>not allowed</u> | <u>(7.15)</u>   | <u> not allowed</u> |
| E   | VAT credit of the month                          | (1.3)              | 0               | (2.6)               |
| F   | opening VAT credit                               | <u> -  </u>        | <u> (1.3)  </u> | <u> (1.3)  </u>     |
| G   | total VAT credit carried forward                 |                    | (1.3)           | (3.9)               |

**Working note**
1. % of export sales

|                            | Baisakh  | jestha    | Ashadh    |
| -------------------------- | -------- | --------- | --------- |
| local sales                | 20       | 5         | 30        |
| export                     | <u>2</u> | <u>50</u> | <u>15</u> |
| total                      | 22       | 55        | 45        |
| % of export (export/total) | 9.09%    | 90.91%    | 33.33%    |

Notes:
1. Baishakh month मा less than 40% export sales छ . Thus, immediate refund पाइन्न . It has to be carried foreward
2. Jestha month मा more than 40% export छ . Thus, immediate refund पाइन्छ of the excess tax of that month. Last month बाट carry forward भएकाे वाला काे immediate refund पाइन्न .

Working Notes (WN)
 * WN1: As per Rule 41(2) of VAT Rule 2053, Tax may be deducted on automobiles on 40% of the cost price.
 * WN2: As per VAT Act 2052, abbreviated tax invoice used for goods, then VAT credit on such goods shall not be allowed.
 * WN3: As per Rule 41(1) of VAT Rule 2053, Tax deduction shall not be allowed for following goods:
   * Beverage
   * Alcohol or alcohol mixed Beverage
   * Petrol mixed vehicles
   * Entertainment expenditure
 * WN4: As per VAT Act, An unregistered person shall not collect VAT and VAT credit shall not be taken for goods on which VAT is not paid.

| Garment local sales                 | 91,000     | 78,000     | 65,000      |
| Total VAT collected (A)             | 91,000     | 78,000     | 65,000      |
|                                     |            |            |             |
| <u> VAT credit/paid </u>            |            |            |             |
| Garment purchase                    | 104,000    | 91,000     | 1,30,000    |
| Ginger purchase                     | N/A        | N/A        | N/A         |
| Total VAT credit (B)                | 104,000    | 91,000     | 1,30,000    |
|                                     |            |            |             |
| Gross Net VAT payable (A - B)       | (13,000)   | (13,000)   | (65,000)    |
| (+) Refund (WN2)                    | 0          | 13,000     | 65,000      |
| (+) Net VAT payable opening balance | -          | (13,000)   | (13,000)    |
| (=) Net VAT payable/(receivable)    | (13,000)   | (13,000)   | (13,000)    |


Working Notes (WN)

WN 1: Taxability of Ginger
As per the Schedule-1 of VAT Act, Ginger is a VAT exempt good. Therefore, no VAT is paid or collected in the purchase or sale of ginger.

WN 2: Calculation of Refund

(Values in Rs. '000')

| Particulars | Jestha | Ashadh | Shrawan |
|---|---|---|---|
| Local Sales (A) |  |  |  |
| - Ginger | 400 | 500 | 600 |
| - Garment | 700 | 600 | 500 |
| Export |  |  |  |
| - Ginger | 100 | 300 | 400 |
| - Garment | 300 | 600 | 1,000 |
| Total Export (B) | 400 | 900 | 1,400 |
| Total Sales (A + B) | 1,500 | 2,000 | 2,500 |
| Percentage (\frac{B}{A+B}) | 26.67% | 45% | 56% |
| Refund | Not allowed | Allowed | Allowed |
 Note: VAT refund is generally applicable if exports exceed a certain percentage of total sales (commonly 40% in many jurisdictions, as seen in the "Allowed" status for Ashadh and Shrawan).
