### Question 8 (page: 45)


Mercantile Ltd. Is providing service in the field of information technology and its sales in the month of Chaitra 2069 was Rs. 5 million. Out of total sales Rs. 3 million was export. The company has got some service from Star Inc. registered and working in USA. Cost paid for service was 2 million and the company paid Rs. 260,000 in IRO as reverse charging payment. Total purchases for the month of Chaitra were 1 million and it has also incurred the following expenses:

| Expenses | Amount (Rs.) |
|---|---|
| Telephone charges | Rs. 10,000 |
| Petrol for vehicle | Rs. 5,000 |
| Printing and stationery | Rs. 5,000 |
The company has taken VAT bill for all the above purchases. You are required to calculate the actual amount of VAT payable by Mercantile Limited for the month of Chaitra 2069.
5 marks-CAP II-June 2012-ICAN

Answer 

Calculation of VAT of Mercantile Ltd.
For the month of Chaitra 2081

| Particulars | Amount | VAT |
|---|---|---|
| VAT Collected |  |  |
| Export sales | 30,00,000 | 0 |
| Local sales | 20,00,000 | 2,60,000 |
| (A) Total VAT payable |  | 2,60,000 |
|  |  |  |
| VAT Credit |  |  |
| Service from USA | 20,00,000 | 2,60,000 |
| Purchases | 10,00,000 | 1,30,000 |
| Telephone charges | 10,000 | 1,300 |
| Petrol for vehicle (WN1) | N/A | — |
| Printing and stationery | 5,000 | 650 |
| (B) Total VAT receivable |  | 3,91,950 |
|  |  |  |
| Net VAT payable / (Receivable) (A - B) |  | (1,31,950) |
Notes from the image:
 * Export Sales: These are zero-rated, hence the VAT is 0.
 * Petrol: As noted by "WN1" (Working Note 1), VAT credit is typically not allowed on petrol for vehicles in many jurisdictions, which is why it is marked as N/A.
 * Result: Since the VAT receivable (Input Tax) is higher than the VAT payable (Output Tax), the company has a net VAT Receivable of 1,31,950.



**Working note -1**

% of export sales = $\frac{\text{3 million}}{\text{5 million}}$
= 60%

Since, the company has export sales of 60% (i.e. > 40%) of total sales it can apply for immediately fund u/s 24(4)

<u>WN2:</u>
Since, VAT is not paid while paying the salary therefore VAT credit cannot be claimed for salary


<u>WN3:</u>
As per the provision of VAT act 2052, schedule 1 goods are VAT exempt goods and electricity is scheduled 1 goods. Therefore, that credit is not taken in electricity expense. 

<u>WN4:</u>
As per Rule 41(1) of VAT Rule 2053, Tax deduction shall not be allowed for following goods:
   * Beverage
   * Alcohol or alcohol mixed Beverage
   * Petrol mixed vehicles
   * Entertainment expenditure

Question 14: 


### Question 14  (page: 46)

ABC limited purchases 200 kg of raw material worth Rs. 1,00,000 plus VAT. It used the raw material to manufacture two items, A (160 kg) and B (40 kg). It also purchased a furniture for Rs. 56,500 (including VAT) to use it as a sale counter. A is a taxable item and B is exempt item. It sold A for Rs. 2,00,000 and exported B for Rs. 1,00,000. Find the ratio at which the credit is to be claimed and VAT payable.



Answer 


Calculation of VAT of ABC Limited
For the month of: — — —

| Particulars                                                                   | Amount   | VAT    |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- | ------ |
| <u>VAT Collected</u>                                                          |          |        |
| Sales of A                                                                    | 2,00,000 | 26,000 |
| Sales of B                                                                    | N/A      | N/A    |
| Total VAT payable                                                             |          | 26,000 |
|                                                                               |          |        |
| <u>VAT credit</u>                                                             |          |        |
| Raw material for A ($160\text{kg}$)                                           | 80,000   | 10,400 |
| Raw material for B ($40\text{kg}$)                                            | N/A      | N/A    |
| Furniture ($\frac{160}{160+40} \times 56,500$)                                |          |        |
| Furniture ($\frac{2,00,000}{1,00,000 + 2,00,000} \times \frac{56,500}{1.13}$) | 33,333   | 4,333  |
| Total VAT receivable                                                          |          | 14,733 |
|                                                                               |          |        |
| Net VAT payable / (receivable)                                                |          | 11,267 |

Key Calculations Noted:
 * VAT on Sales A: Calculated at 13\% of 2,00,000 = 26,000.
 * VAT Credit on Raw Materials: 13\% of 80,000 = 10,400.
 * Proportional VAT on Furniture: The calculation uses a ratio based on sales  to determine the claimable input tax credit.
 * Net Position: Total VAT Payable (26,000) - Total VAT Receivable (14,733) = 11,267 Payable.
Would you like me to verify the mathematical accuracy of these calculations or help you format another page?

Naya Nepal Ltd is engaged in the business of manufacturing juice. The product of the industry has good market in India and Nepal. The company exports the juice to India as well as the same is sold in the local market. Sales turnover of the company for the following quarters are as follows:

|---|---|---|
| Shrawan - Ashoj 2068 | 65 | 85 |
| Kartik - Poush 2068 | 80 | 45 |
| Magh - Chaitra 2068 | 67 | 100 |
| Baisakh - Ashadh 2069 | 136 | 44 |
| Shrawan - Ashoj 2069 | 165 | 85 |
The company is scheduled to export 900M tones of the juice to India and the necessary raw materials are imported at CIF value at Raxaul from South Africa for Rs 80 Lacs. Also the company is scheduled to import raw materials amounting to Rs 20 lacs for the local sales. The company seeks your advice on whether it avails Bank guarantee facility against VAT. If yes what would be the amount of Bank Guarantee required.
10 marks-June 2010-ICAN/December-2009




Answer

According to Sec 8A of VAT act, 2052, industry exporting > 40% of total sales in the last 12 month , may import raw material required for using in production of finished goods to be exported by using bank guarantee facility

In the given case, the company is about to import certain goods for using it in manufacture of goods for export and local purpose

% of export sales during last 12 month (i.e. from kartik 2068, to Ashwin 2069)
$$\frac{80+67+136+165}{45+100+44+85+80+67+136+165}$$
$$\frac{448}{722}$$
$$62.05\%$$
Since, export is > 40% during last 12 months and ==assuming at least 10% value addition for sales is done.== Raw materials worth Rs 80 lakh scheduled to be imported for using it for production of goods for export purpose may be imported by using bank guarantee facility
$\therefore$ Bank guarantee amount → 80 lakh x 13% = Rs 10,40,000

However, Raw materials of Rs 20 lakh can't be imported using bank guarantee facility as it's used for local sales

### VAT directive 1st (page: 69)

VAT Directives: Radheshyam Enterprises has paid Rs 10 Lacs as VAT on purchases in the month of Shrawan 2069. It has collected Rs 1 Lac VAT on sales in the same month. The firm does not have any outstanding liability. In this case, excess tax paid Rs 9 Lacs can be adjusted in the following months. But after all adjustment upto Magh 2069, Rs 2 Lacs still remains as tax credit. The tax payer can claim for refund of remaining amount Rs 2 lacs as per the act and if it is proved after investigation, the same has to be refunded by the tax office.


### VAT directive 2nd (page: 69)

VAT Directives: A registered person after taking permission for issuing abbreviated tax invoice makes the sale amounting to Rs 225,000 including VAT in a day. While making the posting in daily sales entry, the amount of tax is determined by multiplying the tax quotient.
Tax rate 13%, Tax quotient = 13/(13+100) = 13/113
The amount of tax = Rs 2,25,000 X 13/113 = Rs 25,885
In such case, taxable value is Rs 199,115 and tax amount is Rs 25,885. For such registered person, the amount received from sales is Rs 199,115 and VAT amount I Rs 25,885 for that day.

### VAT Directives-1:   (page: 69)

Radheshyam Enterprises has paid Rs 10 Lacs as VAT on purchases in the month of Shrawan 2069. It has collected Rs 1 Lac VAT on sales in the same month. The firm does not have any outstanding liability. In this case, excess tax paid Rs 9 Lacs can be adjusted in the following months. But after all adjustment upto Magh 2069, Rs 2 Lacs still remains as tax credit. The tax payer can claim for refund of remaining amount Rs 2 lacs as per the act and if it is proved after investigation, the same has to be refunded by the tax office.


### VAT Directives:   (page: 69)

A registered person after taking permission for issuing abbreviated tax invoice makes the sale amounting to Rs 225,000 including VAT in a day. While making the posting in daily sales entry, the amount of tax is determined by multiplying the tax quotient.
Tax rate 13%, Tax quotient = 13 / (13 + 100) = 13 / 113
The amount of tax = Rs 2,25,000 \times 13 / 113 = Rs 25,885
In such case, taxable value is Rs 199,115 and tax amount is Rs 25,885. For such registered person, the amount received from sales is Rs 199,115 and VAT amount is Rs 25,885 for that day.

### Question 4 (page:85)

Finance Manager of ABC Hardware is worrying about the price adjustment with it’s supplier. He had heard that he can do the price adjustment through the use of Debit/Credit Note. You are requested to advise him regarding the content of Debit/Credit Note as per VAT Rules.
Answer: As per Rule 20(1) of the Vat Rules, 2053 a person registered under VAT can adjust the price difference on Sale of goods or services by issuing Debit Note or Credit Note. As per the said Rule, the following are the content of Debit/Credit Note:
a. Serial Number
b. Date of Issue
c. Name, Address and PAN Number of Supplier
d. Name, Address and PAN Number (if he is registered) of Receiver
e. Invoice No. and Date of Concerned Transaction
f. Detail of Product or Service and Reason of Debit or Credit
g. Amount of Debit or Credit
h. Vat Debit or Credit amount.
CAP II December 2014

ii) Total payable for computer and printer purchased: Rs. 67,800
iii) Payable made for motorcycle purchased: Rs. 226,000
iv) Payable made to Hotel: Rs. 24,860 for dinner party organized for party meeting.
Find out the VAT amount paid on purchases and collected on sales. Also, workout amount of allowable input Tax credit and VAT payable.

### Question 7 (page:86)

Nepal Philanthropic Society is a “Company not Distributing Profit” registered under the Company Act, 2063. The source of fund of this company comes from membership fees, donation from the member groups and other outsiders. The company deals with various goods other than those mentioned in schedule 1 to VAT Act 2052. Annual turnover of this company ranges from Rs. 60 lakh to 75 lakh every year.
Though profits earned by the company are not for the distribution amongst its members, more than 50% of its profits are utilized towards activities related to the Corporate Social Responsibilities (CSR) every year.
The members of the company when approached by IRD staffs for VAT registration contended that the company need not be registered in VAT. As you are the tax consultant of the entity you are required to comment this statement by the company.
5 marks—July 2015

Answer:

The criteria for the requirement of a company to be registered under VAT Act are not dependent on whether Company distributed its profits or not. If a company fulfills following two conditions, then it needs to be registered under VAT Act.
 * If such Company/entity deals with goods/services that are VAT attractive, and are not exempted as per schedule 1 of the VAT Act.
 * The transaction of such company exceeds the threshold provided under the Act i.e. annual business transactions crosses present taxable limit of 50 lakh.
In the case of our question, both above criteria are attracted; Nepal Philanthropic society is required to be registered under the VAT Act.
Therefore, the statements of the company that it need not be registered under VAT Act do not hold true.





### Question 8 (page:87)

Answer the followings with reference to Value Added Tax Act/Rules, whether the statements are correct or not, with reasons. (5x1=5)

i) While filing an appeal to Revenue Tribunal, the undisputed amount of the assessed Tax due shall have to be deposited and fifty percent of the amount of the Tax in dispute plus the amount of fine shall have to be deposited.
True.
As per section 33 of Value Added Tax Act, 2052, while filing an appeal to Revenue Tribunal, the undisputed amount of the assessed tax due shall have to be deposited and fifty percent of the amount of the tax in dispute plus the amount of fine shall have to be deposited or a bank guarantee for the same has to be furnished.

ii) In case the person has not made the accounts available for inspection under section 16 (1), the penalty amount is Rs. 5,000 for each time.
False.
As per section 29 (1) (ng) in case the person has not made the accounts available for inspection under section 16 (1), the penalty amount is Rs. 20,000 for each time as amended by Finance Act, 2071.

iii) The color of tax registration number plate for registered person shall be green, for unregistered person doing taxable transaction shall be yellow and for person dealing in tax exempted goods and services shall be white.
True.
As per Rule 14(ka) of Value Added Rules, 2053, registered person shall have to place tax registration number plate in the format prescribed by Inland Revenue Department (IRD) in his place of transaction in a clearly visible manner within thirty days from the effective date of VAT rule.
Further, as per circular dated 2062.06.21 by IRD, the color of tax registration number plate for registered person shall be green, for unregistered person doing taxable transaction shall be yellow and for person dealing in tax exempted goods and services shall be white.

iv) Nepal Government is not required to collect Value Added Tax even if it engages in supply of goods or services which attract VAT.
False.
Notwithstanding anything contained in subsection (1) or (2) of section 15, local body or International institution/association/commission based in Nepal or Nepal Government or corporations engaged in non-VAT transactions shall collect value added tax if they engage in supply of goods or services which attract VAT.

v) No Tax is to be levied for a supply of service by a person residing in Nepal to a person outside Nepal who has no business transaction in Nepal.
False.
As per clause 2 of schedule 2 of Value Added Tax, 2052, a supply of service by a person residing in Nepal to a person outside Nepal, who has no business transaction, business representative or legally recognized agent in Nepal, is subjected to zero rate of tax.

### Question No. 10 (page:88)

Sujha Pvt. Ltd. imports the Honda cars and track-laying tractors & sells in Nepal. It has following transactions and information relevant to the VAT calculation in the month of Bhadra, 20X-73.
a. Honda cars sales invoices of Rs. 30 million were issued to VAT registered customers and invoices of Rs. 10 million were issued to individual customers (Not registered in VAT).
b. Track-laying tractors sales invoices totaling Rs. 10 million were issued to individual customers that were not registered for VAT.
c. The company offers a 5% discount for prompt payment.
d. Import price of Honda cars was IRs. 15 million, transportation expense up to custom point was IRs. 100,000 (Paid to Indian transporter) and insurance premium Rs. 100,000 paid to Nepal insurance company. One of the imported cars valued costing Rs. 50 lakhs were used itself for the pickup and drop of its executive director.
e. Import price of Track-laying tractors was IRs. 5 million, transportation expense up to custom point was IRs. 50,000 (Paid to Indian transporter) and insurance premium Rs. 50,000 paid to Nepal insurance company.

All of the figures except IRs are exclusive of VAT.

### Question No. 11 (page:89)
Nepal Surgical House (Pvt) Limited is a principal stockiest for Nepal of Medtronic Inc. Hong Kong (MIH). The company has policy to sell the items to other distributors with 15% markup at landed cost. Medical and surgical equipment manufactured by MIH are subject to both VAT attractive and VAT exempt. MIH makes the shipment by billing on FOB Price and import is done through irrevocable letter of credit. The Company provides following information for the month of Chaitra 2074:
 * a) All items are subject to 5% custom duty under HS code 84.
 * b) Shipment against the LC established in Falgun 2074 having FOB value USD 126,300.00 of which USD 43,500.00 is subject to VAT was released during the month and entire stock was sold on the same month.
 * c) Of the total import of taxable goods during the month, items having FOB Price USD 26,400.00 was revalued to USD 33,100.00 by the custom authorities.
 * d) Terms of Payment 20% during the establishment of LC and 80% on release of documents.
 * e) Effective exchange rate per USD was 102.6, 103.8 & 103.45 during the date of LC establishment, document release and custom clearance respectively.
 * f) Previous non-VAT stock having landed cost of Rs. 2,526,200.00 was also sold during the month.
 * g) The company paid Rs. 146,800.00 inclusive of tax for Birgunj Kathmandu transportation and insurance of the consignment.
Required:
On the basis of above information, prepare VAT statement of Nepal Surgical House (Pvt.) Limited for the month of Chaitra 2074 considering opening VAT credit of Rs. 73,612.00.
(June 2018, 10 Marks)
